# VIctory Research Checklist — NVIDIA RTX 4070 / Ryzen 7 5800X

> **Research-only tracking file.** This is separate from Claude's implementation work. Keep existing Claude notes, tasks, commits, and branches untouched. Research entries are **not** instructions to merge, cherry-pick, or change source automatically. Only mark an item complete when its stated evidence has been recorded.
>
> **Target PC:** RTX 4070 12 GB, Ryzen 7 5800X, 64 GB DDR4-3600. **Reactivated by owner (2026-11-27):** research an adaptive memory manager / automatic GPU-local placement policy; Claude retains implementation authority. This overrides the earlier HOLD note below.
>
> **Historical initial snapshot, superseded by the integration status and Run13 notes below (2026-11-27):** then-`main` was `fc093e2ca81095d9c090fdfe99b79353030c5984`. Experimental branches include `exact-lock`, `loose-lock`, `shader-licm`, `texture-digest`, `gpu-access-costs`, `gpu-access-flags`, `merge-words`, `dstorage-before-write`, and `gta6`. Branches **diverge** from main; a commit's presence on an experiment branch does **not** mean it is deployed on main. Re-check ancestry and latest head before each investigation.

A box below is ticked only when its own "Done when" gate is met: most gates need the game running on the owner's PC, so most items stay open even when their code is merged.

>

>
> **Windows record on the owner's PC (Ryzen 7 5800X, RTX 4070):** release build OK at \`5628dc0\` (all code below); \`cargo test -p victory-gpu --lib -- --test-threads=1\` at \`5628dc0\`: **253 passed, 0 failed, 7 ignored**; \`cargo test -p victory-shader --lib\` on the shader-licm stack: **265 passed, 0 failed** (GPU parity and validation tests on the 4070). Blocks that faulted are left to the kernel for 2 s. New line: stores in place, MB, fell back, not tried. `shadow::MERGE_PARTS` splits "written back" into the fills told (`fill_ahead::wrote`), the own-view store or its try, the old way's guards opened, the old way's writes (with the count of runs of words, `MERGE_RUNS`). **Pads:** the scan line lists every HID device as vendor:product; a device that cannot be opened even without access (HidHide) is named from its path (`sony::path_ids`, USB `VID_054C&PID_0CE6`, Bluetooth `_VID&0002054C_PID&0CE6`, unit-tested), a Sony one said HIDDEN with what to do. | Run 15: the merge line's last four parts, the store line, frames in each phase of `alt`. |

## CURRENT MAIN-BRANCH STATUS — 2026-11-27 07:46 UTC (supersedes earlier branch caveats)

**Verified at [\`9ead5921c64\`](https://github.com/Zaxix/VIctory/commit/9ead5921c649c61d512f3f57503cc0066fc1afaa):** Claude integrated the fxceless GTA6 base and accumulated Zaxix experimental changes onto \`main\`, explicitly retaining the research checklist. The earlier statements below saying that \`main\` contains only \`fc093e2\` or that all optimisation experiments remain branch-only are **historical**, not the current state. Do not cherry-pick experimental branch commits as if missing. The old upstream-specific \`fc093e2\` heap-cap implementation was *not* transplanted unchanged: the GTA6 base handles the cap via its own \`probe_view_limit\` path. Inspect code before attributing a specific commit's diff to this version.

**Verified at [\`cf4a43b\`](https://github.com/Zaxix/VIctory/commit/cf4a43bda36b4fe6fbd06d316a22fba703287e9c):** levels-on-demand GPU-visible texture views are now rebuilt by default only at a submission-side idle point; alternatives \`VICTORY_VIEWS_REMADE_QUIET=0|1\` remain. This addresses the **source-level candidate** in R20 but **does not prove** 30–50-minute device losses are fixed. R20/R16 remain pending NVIDIA long-run verification. The submit path records that a forced idle/sweep may occur after 1,500 ms; that only proves safe immediate descriptor writes under its assumptions, **not** that all previously recorded but unsubmitted lists can be repointed to relocated buffers.

**Claude review of R23–R27:** existing main implementation already has DXGI budget-conscious texture queues, fixed 1 MiB minimum/1,024 MiB card-buffer pool, card/target room reservations, read/write access counters, opt-in \`VICTORY_WINDOW_UPLOAD=1\` and opt-in in-place mapping. Current missing parts are *per-resource cost estimates*, an adaptive admission policy, safe runtime migration and explicit MakeResident/Evict policy. **Do not assume explicit residency control is needed**: benchmark budgets/pressure first. User has confirmed Resizable BAR on, but run the existing window-upload switch in a controlled A/B before building new placement. Code is now on \`main\`, but **no RTX 4070 in-game validation by this research assistant**.

**Next-step decision:** P0 long-run removal root cause first; then measurement-only per-resource telemetry for R23. Only attempt migration after correct ownership and evidence. A GPU-idle point is not a substitute for the **recorded-but-unsubmitted-list** resource-generation check. Do not discount same-byte CPU/GPU races as harmless: the emulator must maintain the actual guest's observable ordering and never overwrite later host CPU writes with stale GPU data.

> **Goal: large, measured world-frame-time improvements, without requiring a game run after every small patch.** Latest audited `main` before this research commit was [b9b61c0](https://github.com/Zaxix/Defeat/commit/b9b61c09b3d7cfea963f99df9ed325c8584f2891), containing Claude's **[0c62860 R64 offline implementation](https://github.com/Zaxix/Defeat/commit/0c62860371d9a9212c74964f5725d489235e56e7)** and **[490577d runstats](https://github.com/Zaxix/Defeat/commit/490577de6f13388acd5b295b58e3f4e3e95005c7)**. **Run15 at `be0ed3b7` remains the latest owner gameplay evidence inspected**, not an FPS benchmark of 2546250/0c62860. Gemini's pasted deep-research report explicitly had no Defeat source access: treat its architecture projections as hypotheses, not verified missing features. Claude owns source and implementation-status table.

> **R66 NEWEST RESEARCH-ONLY CHECKPOINT (2026-11-28):** Since R65, Claude committed [4a8acbe](https://github.com/Zaxix/Defeat/commit/4a8acbe7189997403287e4d4925348692adb5118) to `main`: release-build `VICTORY_CARD_METER=1` now collects results when the frame logger is off; `VICTORY_READBACK_REUSE=1` adds an **opt-in observer only** of repeated own-view write-backs; and `cargo xtask runstats` can report card-running time, slow-storage stage breakdowns, and reuse counts. **Do not ask Claude to implement these again.** No owner Run16 gameplay result, FPS win, or successful local Windows validation of this newest commit was supplied to this research pass. The R65 board remains the general ordering; the **specific R66 gates below are the current next actions**. Run15's provided CSV/JSON pack is evidence from executable `be0ed3b7`, NOT from `4a8acbe`.

> **R72 RUN17 SOURCE-AND-LOG AUDIT (2026-11-28):** **3k+ draw window means Run17 N57 p50 169.6ms / p90 280.0ms vs Run16 N60 168.0 / 336.5ms; not matched scenes, no speed claim**. Real concerns: sampled texture-warning lines hide **>=109,001** unannounced changes, **13,000** attempted updates of resident textures with no room for new ones, **26** Wave64 rejected host pipelines including two water programs, **1.245M guest view slots vs 1M host cap**, **298MB** worst-sampled DXGI local slack and **204** >1s DirectStorage requests (different run). CPU submission/guards/copyback still expensive. See [R72](#r72--run-17-full-log-audit-coherence-missing-pipelines-gpu-idle-and-streaming-2026-11-28-research-only) and new separate detailed Claude TXT. **Research-only: do not change source without Claude's guarded validation.**

> **R71 NATIVE-FPS SOURCE FORENSICS (2026-11-28):** [VIctory-NVIDIA](https://github.com/trustno1-coder/VIctory-NVIDIA) ~20-30fps **rendered** is not a frame-gen gain. NVIDIA author 26–30 opening rooms **RTX5070/9800X3D, Series X 1440p DLSS Quality, RT OFF, FG separate**; independent 17.8 rendered FPS **DLSS Performance+FG, counted separately**; foxhound RTX4070 SUPER/7800X3D 27–30 opening, 21 water, newer 20min driving p50 33.3ms with **DLSS Quality, FG OFF**. **No matched DLSS-OFF/FG-OFF 20fps test or user RTX4070/5800X test verified.** Defeat **already has** GPU copy/hash/compare; don't port redundant feature. Source-level differentials: **12GB buffer residency NVIDIA ~18% up to 2048MiB min16MiB vs Defeat 1024MiB min1MiB;** NVIDIA comments report huge GPU pass improvements, but Defeat Run16 ~352MiB minimum sampled VRAM slack means no blind growth. **Defeat lacks NVIDIA `list/thin.rs`**, potential recorder cost; profile GPU idle and descriptor locks first. NVIDIA `WC_NO_BACK` trades readback correctness; **do not blindly skip**. Full R71 below; detailed owner-deliverable TXT handoff in chat.

Defeat **does not yet have** its FidelityFX host import interposer / title Series X upscaler-path patch / Streamline proxy; current GPU D3D12 presenter and HLE resolver exist. **Native FPS != interpolated displayed FPS**. Prioritize offline upscaler contract, exact visual/queue ordering, DLSS SR, and only then optional DLSS FG with Reflex and 12GB VRAM gate; FSR3 and XeSS cross-vendor fallback after a working backend. **Research-only Claude/Gemini handoff [R70](#r70--dlss-super-resolution-and-frame-generation-multi-vendor-feasibility-2026-11-28-research-only).**

> **R69 latest Gemini-report source audit (2026-11-28):** Gemini's pasted Kelvaroq/NVIDIA comparison was inspected and **reverified against live GitHub**, including previously claimed-inaccessible [Kelvaroq README](https://github.com/stellarr-studio/kelvaroq/blob/main/README.md) and [French architecture notes](https://github.com/stellarr-studio/kelvaroq/blob/main/documentation.md). A **genuinely distinct P0 research lead** is [Kelvaroq patch 0038](https://github.com/stellarr-studio/kelvaroq/blob/main/native/patches/0038-direct-constants.patch): eligible descriptor-table constants read straight from an ordered GPU host copy instead of being filled from guest memory on the CPU at submit time. Defeat's `draw.rs` still calls `defer_fill` for general table constants; `VICTORY_CARD_FILLS` is a narrower, OFF-by-default experiment already measured **worse** (+11ms frame, +6ms GPU), so **do not simply switch it on**. Also **already built in Defeat**: mesh amplification launch, wide GPU card hashes, in-process plain copy and optional asynchronous present (which earlier Defeat tests found no gain). Kelvaroq's headline 22ms is **GPU work/floor** from an unshipped experimental memory path, NOT current public 30fps gameplay. New checks and anti-duplication details: [R69](#r69--gemini-kelvaroq-and-victory-nvidia-technical-fact-check-and-fps-experiment-queue-2026-11-28-research-only).

> **R68 newer research (2026-11-28):** The closest hardware lead is [fxceless RTX 4070 25-minute open-world report](https://github.com/fxceless/victory-fork/blob/main/README.md) of ~17 native FPS with its adopted NVIDIA fork (unmatched scenes), and [foxhound RTX 4070 SUPER report](https://github.com/trustno1-coder/VIctory-NVIDIA/blob/main/docs/README-foxhound.md) of 27–30 native FPS in opening scenes with ray tracing OFF and approximated indirect lighting. These are **author reports, not Defeat test results or a promised speedup**. The strongest nonduplicate source difference is NVIDIA's [`VICTORY_THIN`](https://github.com/trustno1-coder/VIctory-NVIDIA/blob/main/crates/victory-gpu/src/list/thin.rs) off-thread command-list translation; also check its guarded-write strategy, texture heap pooling and small-buffer ahead. **Measure Defeat's same-site critical path and visible-fidelity differences first; no blanket cherry-pick.** Current prioritized evidence and Claude/Gemini handoff: [R68](#r68--cross-emulator-native-fps-research-two-user-linked-forks-and-prioritized-porting-gates-2026-11-28-research-only).

> **R67 LIVE RESULTS (2026-11-28; newer than the historical R66 snapshot below):** Owner provided **`run16.zip` (`run16.txt`), `gpu16.csv`** and overlapping text GPU telemetry. The emulator log explicitly reports **source `3665d56781bc`** (post-`4a8acbe`; research-only R66 commit) with **`VICTORY_BVH_BUILDER=0`**; no `VICTORY_READBACK_REUSE=1` or `VICTORY_CARD_METER=1` was logged. **Run16 is now observed**, but only as a non-scene-matched, single-session performance sample: for >=3k draws, **N=60, 168.0ms p50 / 336.5ms p90**, versus Run15 **N=67, 203.4 / 278.4ms**. Copy-back median **43.3 vs 76.0ms**, but the frame **p90 REGRESSED**. Priority is now **(1) tail-latency root cause and matched-scene visual A/B, (2) guard/DirectStorage/submitter critical path, (3) isolated readback-observer evidence**; no causal FPS win or safety of deferred memory writes has been proven. See [R67](#r67--run16-owner-log-and-gpu-audit-versus-run15-next-claudegemini-research-gates-2026-11-28-research-only).

| Order | Workstream | State and immediate engineering gate |
|---:|---|---|
| **1 · P0** | **Verify the post-Run15 runtime code and measure true CPU/GPU critical path** | Run16 warm scene on the integrated main, `VICTORY_BVH_BUILDER=0`, `VICTORY_DIRTY_4K=0`, normal `VICTORY_WRITE_PLAIN=1`. Measure p50/p90 wall-frame, copy-back, guard-lock caller hold/wait, storage before/after guest page writes and GPU queue idle. Use existing `VICTORY_CARD_METER` timestamps; `VICTORY_GPU_TIMING=1` is a **separate short diagnostics-only experiment** because it executes each list separately with waits and changes FPS. Run16 at source `3665d56781bc` has now been supplied; its unmatched scene, picture and tail-latency gates are in R67. |
| **2 · P0/P1** | **Producer/consumer trace of GPU-written guest memory BEFORE lazy readback** | Gemini's biggest potentially architectural idea is *avoid GPU→CPU transfers the guest never consumes before overwrite*. Existing card copy-back, `watch::writing`, guest physical aliases and queue-fence tracking mean **do not implement `GPU_OWNED` page-fault ownership or discard blindly**. First add optional nonproprietary address-generation/fence/consumer tracing: GPU write → readback copy → first title CPU read / storage mapping / subsequent GPU overwrite. Record observed fraction truly dead or deferred; use a deterministic alias and multi-queue synthetic fixture. Only develop a lazy path if title CPU-read interception and exact render/state parity are provable. |
| **3 · P0** | **Guard notification and DirectStorage queue serialization** | New `watch.rs::guards_line` per-caller waits/holds and repaired `dstorage.rs` slowest phase logger exist, plus [R64](#r64--between-game-run-fps-development-batch-and-cross-emulator-technique-audit-research-only-2026-11-28) 20k-request scheduler test. Run15's conditional ~2.26s worker queue wait does not prove 8–1024-lane scheduler selection is expensive; synthetic selection stayed <200µs. Use next trace to distinguish lock wait, `VirtualProtect` hold, physical page retries, stalled worker and guest status FIFO before modifying. Consider opt-in batching only if an exact guest-before/after-write ordering model proves it safe. |
| **4 · P1** | **GPU submission dependency graph and safe earlier work** | Defeat already has `VICTORY_ASYNC_SUBMIT`, `VICTORY_PARALLEL_REFRESH`, `VICTORY_QUEUE2` (two copy lanes) and D3D12 list recording. Map per-thread list-ready→submit→direct/copy fences and readback dependencies; synthetic D3D12 timeline tests for early kicks before any frame-N/N−1 pipelining. No unverified claim of large safe parallelism or 10–20FPS. |
| **5 · P1** | **Maximize existing GPU-upload heap path only when it is the true bottleneck** | **Already implemented:** `host.rs::gpu_upload_buffer` uses `D3D12_HEAP_TYPE_GPU_UPLOAD`; `shadow.rs::window_upload_for` uses it conditionally, and `VICTORY_WINDOW_UPLOAD` defaults ON by code. RTX4070 capability reported. Measure eligible window fallbacks and LOCAL budget and benchmark only specific CPU-write→GPU-read dynamic resources. **GPU_UPLOAD is not GPU→CPU READBACK**, and forcing GPU-local allocation under pressure may hurt. |
| **6 · Safety/deferred** | **Shader/BVH, speculative page protections and four-switch bundles** | BVH builder stays OFF for FPS baseline; five shadows still have no real binds, Wave64 incompatibilities remain. **Do NOT enable deferred `VirtualProtect`, sharded/lock-free alias tables, GPU-ownership lazy readback, GPU diff compaction and multiframe pipelining all together**. RPCS3's deferred-protection issue records visual regressions; each needs its own independent byte/fence/alias proof and opt-in rollback. `VICTORY_DIRTY_4K` remains OFF pending hair/skin parity. |

**Between-game-run recommendation:** Claude can keep working now: (A) synthetic **GPU readback consumer-lifetime histogram** and address-generation trace, (B) CPU/GPU queue-ready-to-submit timeline analyzer extending existing `cargo xtask runstats`, (C) guard/open/alias lock-order model for alternative batching, (D) candidate-specific GPU-upload buffer microbenchmark only if diagnostic counters show meaningful CPU upload/copy work. Do not redo R64 fixed-seed word-run/write-back tests or DirectStorage scheduling fixtures. Then perform **one controlled game Run16** for accurate FPS; no synthetic fixture can establish a sustained game gain. See [R65](#r65--gemini-performance-deep-research-audited-against-latest-defeat-and-cross-emulator-sources-2026-11-28).

**Historical priority table below (ARCHIVED R00–R40 rank labels):** preserved for traceability, **not the live implementation order**. The current FPS-first R62 board above (rechecked in R63) overrides its earlier order, but the detailed evidence gates below remain valid unless a later R-section expressly supersedes them. This is a research queue, not permission for source edits, cherry-picks, merges or PRs; Claude retains implementation ownership.

- **P0 / BLOCKER:** a correctness or stability failure, or measurement prerequisite without which performance comparisons are unreliable.
- **P1 / HIGH ROI:** code already implemented experimentally or a measured candidate with a low-cost controlled A/B.
- **P2 / CONDITIONAL:** meaningful improvement but depends on bottleneck, precise synchronization or more complex correctness verification.
- **P3 / RESEARCH:** useful later, not justified as the next engineering edit.
- **P1/P2 ADAPTIVE TRACK ACTIVE:** the owner reactivated research on GPU-local placement. Its observability/correctness design is P1; enabling automatic migration is P2 and gated on stability and benchmark evidence.

**Evidence status:** V = verified repository source, C = contributor test/claim (not rerun here), H = proposed hypothesis, T = tested on the owner's exact PC (currently *none verified by this research checklist*). An experimental commit or a type-check is **not T**. Likely effect and work estimate below are **qualitative judgments**, not measured FPS.

**Decision gates:** if a switch changes GPU ms but not actual release frame ms, mark its effect **GPU-only**, not an FPS win; if an optimisation changes draw output, corrupts water, skips data, or causes device removal, revert the experiment and keep the research record. Run distinct A/Bs on the *same* guest mode, game build, settings, driver, shader-cache state and near-identical camera/draw workload. Use at least three comparable samples per candidate where feasible.

## Working agreement / evidence gate

- [x] **R00 — Confirm access and inventory.** Repository, README, architecture, and branch names inspected (2026-11-27); target PC recorded.
- [ ] For every proposed fix, record: repository + branch + commit SHA; observed issue and exact reproduction; profiling data; relevant tests; Windows/NVIDIA build result; side-by-side visual correctness; whether change is already present on target branch.
- [ ] Maintain three statuses separately: **researched**, **implemented on a branch**, **verified on the owner's PC**. Do not equate code review or a Linux/cfg(windows) type-check with a Windows build or working in-game.
- [ ] **R64 standing research rule (owner request):** in each substantive optimization research batch, review source-level techniques in active adjacent emulators (at least Xenia Canary, RPCS3, shadPS4 and xemu where relevant), compare each with Defeat's current implementation, record original link and constraints, and label *already exists / worthwhile prototype / not applicable / correctness risk*. Do not promise FPS from another console or paste incompatible/licence-unreviewed source into Defeat. Only update research-owned portions of this checklist; Claude owns implementation.
- [ ] Use one reproducible world scene / camera / console profile / resolution / driver / warm shader cache across comparisons. Log median and p95 frame time, CPU prepare, GPU execution, copy-back, upload bytes, memory/VRAM budgets, and crashes.
- [ ] Avoid speculative merges, broad cherry-picks, and combining competing changes without an ancestry + conflict + correctness audit. Claude owns all source changes and PRs.

## P0 — Establish a baseline and identify the critical path

- [ ] **R01 — Capture the RTX 4070 / 5800X baseline on the actual branch to be tested.** Record current SHA, NVIDIA driver, Windows version, firmware/Resizable BAR state (observed, not assumed), Series X vs S mode, game build, resolution, and shader-cache warmth. Measure at least three consistent runs. **Done when:** reproducible baseline frame-time table + settings + logs exist.
- [ ] **R02 — Separate CPU time from GPU and synchronization time.** Trace per-frame command preparation, list worker waits, texture hashing, detiling, actual upload, copy-back, fence waits, and draw/dispatch work. `VICTORY_GPU_TIMING=1` and GPU-based validation alter timing and must *not* be treated as normal release FPS measurements. **Done when:** critical-path timeline identifies the top two measured costs. Source: [README debugging flags](../README.md).
- [x] **R03 — Compare main with each experiment branch by commit ancestry / scoped diff.** *(Done 2026-11-27, Claude: every experiment branch is an ancestor of `main` at `9ead592`; see the implementation status table at the top.)* Record which experimental fixes are absent from main, which overlap, and which have been superseded. **Done when:** a branch-by-branch table shows `SHA → files → tests → result → target status`. Do not assume a merged upstream fix is absent until source checked.

## P1 — CPU-side submission, resource creation and locks

- [ ] **R04 — Investigate `shadow::EXACT` lock contention and buffer lifecycle.** Experimental commit [`16183bd7`](https://github.com/Zaxix/VIctory/commit/16183bd7c578f175beab7c076d6c5eba0346fedd) moves allocation/release outside the lock and throttles prune scans, but **does not** port fence-safe buffer recycling from Belobug PR #451. Profile waits, resource creation rate, duplicate-on-race resolution, outstanding GPU references, and release timing. **Done when:** cold/warm scene performance + fence lifetime tests pass on Windows/NVIDIA. Research: [Belobug PR #451](https://github.com/Belobug/VIctory-Belobug-AMD/pull/451).
- [ ] **R05 — Investigate `shadow::LOOSE` lock contention and overlapping windows.** Experimental commit [`dc744dbb`](https://github.com/Zaxix/VIctory/commit/dc744dbb8d5a4013176c31b324d7801e9a99cc80) changes the mutex to an `RwLock` and builds missed windows outside it. Verify overlap semantics, cleanup and concurrent lookup/insert; measure on Ryzen 5800X. **Done when:** p95 lock wait and resulting frame-time delta are reported. Reference: [Belobug PR #443](https://github.com/Belobug/VIctory-Belobug-AMD/pull/443).
- [ ] **R06 — Command-list submission and indirect dispatch.** Inspect command signature selection, transient buffer allocations, per-list setup, residency waits, list reuse and worker utilization. Evaluate fxceless's lean indirect dispatch independently of memory changes. **Done when:** GPU vs CPU timings both logged; no missing indirect work. Reference: [fxceless PR #1352](https://github.com/fxceless/victory-gta6/pull/1352) (verify repository/PR availability).
  - **Implementation status, 2026-11-27:** [`spread-lean-nvidia`](https://github.com/Zaxix/VIctory/tree/spread-lean-nvidia) at [`f41357b6`](https://github.com/Zaxix/VIctory/commit/f41357b6f32a052d9cf5b397d0214adbb757aff3) is **one commit ahead of `texture-digest`** (verified GitHub compare), **not merged into `main`**. The changed files are `README.md`, `crates/victory-gpu/src/draw.rs`, and `crates/victory-gpu/src/host.rs`. The added default selects NVIDIA by host adapter vendor ID `0x10DE` and honors `VICTORY_SPREAD_LEAN=0` (off), `=1` (on), and `=alt` (alternating). Code inspected; Claude reports type-check only, **not built or run on Windows/NVIDIA**. Original indirect dispatch logic existed in the base; this commit primarily changes default selection. The external RTX 3060/5080/5060 figures in the commit message are contributor reports, **not measurements on the owner's PC**.
  - **Pending validation:** on RTX 4070 run `cargo build --release -p victory` and `cargo test -p victory-gpu --lib indirect` with the branch checked out. Specifically exercise the 100,000-group indirect case and compare forced `VICTORY_SPREAD_LEAN=0` vs `=1` in the same world scene (warm caches, identical profile); record frame-time percentiles, CPU prepare, GPU time, indirect draw/dispatch counts, visual captures and device-loss logs. Do not mark R06 complete before this.


## P1 — Texture hashing, detiling and streaming

- [ ] **R07 — Measure unchanged-texture checks separately from transfers.** Experimental [`63b87e8`](https://github.com/Zaxix/VIctory/commit/63b87e8ca5e26f9c20194ea576fd7d93281a25f3) uses XXH3-128 with `VICTORY_TEXTURE_DIGEST=blake3` comparison and logs bytes checked vs unchanged. Check changed bytes, remap/unmap races, CPU cost, and content correctness. **Done when:** controlled blake3 vs XXH3 measurements and race regression tests are recorded. References: [upstream issue #916](https://github.com/Marvisak/VIctory/issues/916), [#3269](https://github.com/Marvisak/VIctory/issues/3269).
- [ ] **R08 — Profile detiling, texture invalidation and actual CPU→GPU uploads.** Do not count bytes read for digesting as upload bandwidth. Determine whether partial-tile refresh or content-aware invalidation saves the measured critical path, without skipping legitimate writes. **Done when:** hashed/detiled/uploaded byte and ms counters reconcile, screenshots match baseline. References: [#915](https://github.com/Marvisak/VIctory/issues/915), [#916](https://github.com/Marvisak/VIctory/issues/916).
- [ ] **R09 — DirectStorage/memory-write ordering.** Review the experimental `dstorage-before-write` and `merge-words` branches, ensure writes invalidate/refresh mirrored data in correct order. **Done when:** deterministic storage-write/readback regression scenarios pass; no stale texture or geometry data. Reference: [upstream issue #2503](https://github.com/Marvisak/VIctory/issues/2503).

## P1 — NVIDIA GPU correctness and shader execution

- [ ] **R10 — Validate capped NVIDIA shader-visible descriptor heaps.** Main includes [`fc093e2`](https://github.com/Zaxix/VIctory/commit/fc093e2ca81095d9c090fdfe99b79353030c5984), restoring usable space for host-copy view pairs. Confirm the actual descriptor budget, dynamic slot exhaustion, remap behavior, null views, and GPU validation on the RTX 4070; avoid duplicating this fix. **Done when:** no descriptor exhaustion, out-of-range accesses, or silently dropped view updates over a long world run.
- [ ] **R11 — Evaluate DXIL LICM and barrier elimination from the experiment branch.** [`2293f308`](https://github.com/Zaxix/VIctory/commit/2293f3082162fb492558784896bf3cb24688d9d0) ports optimization passes (passes 256/512 in this tree). Run shader tests, DXIL validation and controlled GPU-time comparisons. Protect IEEE/NaN, memory ordering, wave64-on-wave32 semantics, water and geometry correctness. **Done when:** shader hashes, validation rate, GPU time and side-by-side reference captures show no new failures. Source: [klbf-lab PR #41](https://github.com/PastiiJay/klbf-lab/pull/41).
- [ ] **R12 — Identify truly slow translated shaders.** Correlate logged hash + guest pipeline + descriptor inputs + dispatch dimensions, then compare generated DXIL and console instructions. Evaluate specific optimizations *only after* confirming where GPU time is spent; never mask a bad buffer input by blindly shortening loops. **Done when:** a top-N shader-cost table and per-shader correctness tests exist.

## P2 — GPU synchronization and rendering defects

- [ ] **R13 — Fence-safe recycling and copy-back concurrency.** Cross-check [PR #2112](https://github.com/Marvisak/VIctory/pull/2112), [PR #3102](https://github.com/Marvisak/VIctory/pull/3102), and Belobug [#451](https://github.com/Belobug/VIctory-Belobug-AMD/pull/451). Trace CPU writes during GPU readback, queue dependency ordering, release/reuse only after fence completion, and device removal. **Done when:** targeted race tests survive parallel CPU writes, repeated remaps and asynchronous GPU completion. *This task is fence/copy-back correctness; adaptive placement is separately tracked in R23–R27.*
- [ ] **R14 — Ray-traced shadows / `XboxBvhBuilder` → host DXR.** Determine what builder and rehydration calls are actually used, whether acceleration structures are constructed and barriers correct, and why shadows stay black. Do not confuse ambient-light approximation with working ray-traced shadows. **Done when:** trace + host validation + matching game capture confirm working structures, or the exact missing operation is isolated.
- [ ] **R15 — Wave64/format/occlusion correctness.** Verify NVIDIA wave32 fallback, unsupported typed UAV format handling, occlusion query semantics and depth bounds. Main already contains a [null-view crash fix](https://github.com/Zaxix/VIctory/commit/219878a0e7754511d89306331812971bb2153f5f) and [typed-UAV guard](https://github.com/Zaxix/VIctory/commit/ea488a9f9901e4375c547a57795ae8cb79d577a4); avoid reimplementing. **Done when:** captured output, draw counts, validator logs and regression cases match expected behaviour.

## P2 — Long-run reliability and optional rendering profile

- [ ] **R16 — Long-duration NVIDIA stability.** Repeat 30–60+ minute runs with DRED enabled, record GPU resets, live resource and descriptor counts, residency changes, fence waits and cumulative memory. Compare short clean run vs late-session state. **Done when:** a specific failure site or stable non-regressing run is documented.
- [ ] **R17 — Series S mode on NVIDIA as a separately labelled experiment.** Confirm the actual render profile, supported output/UAV formats and upscaler path. Report Series X vs S frame times only under otherwise matched scene/settings; do not compare RX 7900 XTX Series S scores with RTX 4070 Series X results as equivalent. **Done when:** paired correctness/FPS measurements exist.

## P2 — Scarlett executable cross-reference (no runtime claims)

- [ ] **R18 — Relate five guest memory regions to GPU usage.** Use the already reported `0x01E86440` function RVA as a **build-specific analysis lead only**. Verify it against the exact Scarlett executable checksum, reconstruct caller-controlled region sizes, map source/destination permissions and all descriptor creations. Determine whether any of the three conjectured read-only pools receives a writable GPU UAV **with proof**, not assumptions. **Done when:** per-region allocation/writer/descriptor evidence table exists.
- [ ] **R19 — Connect guest GPU calls to VIctory performance events.** Correlate reported `XboxBvhBuilder`, texture streaming and `XMemTransferMemory` call chains with actual emulator trace/queue timing, not raw disassembly alone. **Done when:** a guest operation → host command → time/correctness link is reproducible.


## CROSS-REFERENCED RESEARCH NOTES — 2026-11-27 (source-backed hypotheses, not instructions to port blindly)

### R20 / P0 — In-flight descriptor update hazard: a precise new hypothesis for long-run removal

- **Verified in this fork:** \`crates/victory-gpu/src/descriptor/host.rs\` method \`HostHeap::texture_views_remade\` states that live GPU-visible descriptor slots can be rewritten when \`host::submissions_in_flight() > 0\`. The code counts \`VIEWS_REMADE[1]\` ("remade while a submission was in flight"), and has an existing **opt-in** \`VICTORY_VIEWS_REMADE_QUIET=1\` mode that defers those remakes. This is a *real code-level risk candidate*, not proof it caused any of the 30–50-minute device losses.
- **Cross-reference:** Microsoft's D3D12 guidance requires applications to manage synchronization and descriptor/resource lifetimes explicitly. [Executing and synchronizing command lists](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/direct3d12/executing-and-synchronizing-command-lists), [Direct3D12 resource barriers](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/desktop/direct3d12/using-resource-barriers-to-synchronize-resource-states-in-direct3d-12). fxceless reports intermittent \`0x887A0005\` losses despite its unrelated short-session fixes: [fork README](https://github.com/fxceless/victory-fork/blob/main/README.md).
- **Smallest decisive experiment:** retain the same branch and world scene; log \`VIEWS_REMADE[1]\`, completed/started queue submission IDs, descriptor slot, associated resource generation and DRED removal reason. Compare \`VICTORY_VIEWS_REMADE_QUIET=0\` vs \`=1\` under *separate* long runs. Note that quiet mode can delay texture updates, so compare screenshots and blank slots too. Repeat with D3D12 debug/GPU validation separately; those modes themselves affect timing. A disappearance of crashes in one run is not sufficient proof; repeat.
- **If confirmed — design hypothesis:** do not mutate a descriptor slot that any queued/in-flight list may still read. Version the host descriptor allocation per in-flight use (or defer a slot's rewrite until its **last referencing queue fence** completes); retain the previous resource and descriptor until the same fence. A global idle wait is a correctness control, not necessarily a performant final design. Prove that old and new descriptors never alias in a live list, and validate zero stale-resource reads.
- **Related separate check:** fxceless [commit \`2ba6e1f\`](https://github.com/fxceless/victory-fork/commit/2ba6e1f99844e3765d66e7710e02e541daa0f9b4) caps CBVs at the host's 64 KiB limit after an NVIDIA \`CreateConstantBufferView\` invalid-call report. **Do not assume this is absent or present in the target branch**: inspect \`constant_view_size\`/CBV creation and already-existing capped-descriptor code before acting. Distinguish CBV-invalid-call failures from descriptor-in-flight races and from the main heap-slot cap fix.
- **Crash evidence to collect:** reason (\`DXGI_ERROR_DEVICE_REMOVED\`, \`DEVICE_HUNG\` or \`INVALID_CALL\`), DRED breadcrumbs/page-fault result, last complete submission, allocation names/IDs, descriptor slot writes, live resource/heap counts, driver/event log, VRAM budget, and time-to-failure. No reported page fault does **not** exonerate invalid GPU commands or synchronization bugs. Microsoft's [DRED spec](https://microsoft.github.io/DirectX-Specs/d3d/DeviceRemovedExtendedData.html) explains limitations. If the specific fork supports \`VICTORY_D3D12_DRED=faults|light\`, \`faults\` captures page-fault information with less tracing and \`light\` includes breadcrumbs (fxceless [\`4cc775f\`](https://github.com/fxceless/victory-fork/commit/4cc775f57839907a4ae932bc0a9e08cd3b2e0781)); check exact branch implementation first.

- [ ] **R20 — Prove or rule out descriptor writes into live/in-flight GPU-visible slots.** Record \`VIEWS_REMADE[1]\` and consecutive long-run on/off results, plus DRED/context and screenshot correctness; if proved, require fence-based deferred rewrite/retirement tests before considering the fix complete.

### R21 / P1 — Submission thread spent on COM destruction and retirement bookkeeping

- **Verified from target fork source:** \`crates/victory-gpu/src/host.rs\` documents **36.8 million** host-object retirements across about 240 seconds in one fxceless-source test (run 313), mostly repeated descriptor writes. It already has \`VICTORY_RETIRE_LEAN=1|alt\` to avoid retiring the previous reference when the *same COM object* is retained, and \`VICTORY_REAPER=1|alt\` to move eligible destruction to another worker. This is a recorded measurement in imported code, **not a benchmark on this owner's Ryzen**.
- **Cross-reference:** \`shadow::EXACT\` [\`16183bd7\`](https://github.com/Zaxix/VIctory/commit/16183bd7c578f175beab7c076d6c5eba0346fedd) moved allocations/releases outside its lock; \`shadow::LOOSE\` [\`dc744dbb\`](https://github.com/Zaxix/VIctory/commit/dc744dbb8d5a4013176c31b324d7801e9a99cc80) similarly removes driver calls from its lock. If CPU submission is still busy after those fixes, retirement traffic is a distinct plausible remaining bottleneck.
- **Measure:** descriptor rewrites/second, same-object rewrites/second, retirement queue depth and bytes, CPU ms spent inside \`bury\`/\`reap\`/\`resource::let_go_idle\`, lock wait, highest-latency tail. Use on/off/alternating variants already present, **one flag at a time**, compare p95 frame latency and GPU completed-submission watermark. A win in lock wait alone is not enough.
- **If confirmed — design hypothesis:** replace per-retirement global-vector append/sort with a **monotonic fence-bucketed retirement queue** and batched completed-epoch draining. Drop COM objects after their *last* referencing submission completes; keep the previous object alive until then, including if a descriptor points at it. Keep destruction outside locks. Avoid "early release" merely because one copy of the pointer was replaced: another live descriptor or list may still reference it. Verify races under multithreaded recording and forced device removal.
- [ ] **R21 — Record whether retirement/object destruction actually dominates CPU submission** and compare the existing isolated flags on the owner's machine before proposing any queue rewrite.

### R13 / P2 — Fence-safe buffer recycling is **not** just a power-of-two free list

- **Verified source/review:** Belobug [PR #451](https://github.com/Belobug/VIctory-Belobug-AMD/pull/451) quarantines recycled \`shadow::exact()\` buffers, rounds to a size bucket, zeroes stale reused contents, and limits memory in the pool. An NVIDIA contributor reports an RTX 4080 test at **276 → 142 ms/frame** in one fork context (review comment on #451), not reproduced here.
- **Critical review nuance:** a reviewer states \`VICTORY_EXACT_POOL_MB=0\` does **not** reproduce the baseline algorithm (bucket rounding, two-frame eviction and host retirement are different even with pooling disabled). The same reviewer notes an interval between the submitted/queued watermark sample and descriptor-only use, where the **two-frame delay rather than proven fence ownership** protects reuse. Device tests passed on AMD GPUs; performance and the title workload were *not* tested in that review.
- **If adapting to NVIDIA:** index reuse safety by **the maximum GPU queue-fence value that can still reference the particular buffer**, plus an explicit "not yet submitted" state; don't graduate by wall-clock/frame count alone. A resource reused under the same GPU virtual address must have the required bytes reinitialized; verify all out-of-range/tail reads return zeros, including when a 64 KiB-old view becomes a shorter one. Avoid unbounded quarantine memory, and preserve the ability to retire on pressure.
- **Test plan:** create buffer A, record descriptor reference on a deferred list, evict A, allocate B with the same bucket, submit A and B in adversarial orders, then check bytes and DRED; repeat across queues, list reset, cancellation and device removal. Compare against the **true old implementation**, not just \`POOL_MB=0\`. This is a new design proposal; no claim of a tested fix.

### R07 + R08 / P1/P2 — What texture metrics **actually** count

- **Upstream counter correction:** issue [#3269](https://github.com/Marvisak/VIctory/issues/3269) reports 344–444 MB/frame as "texture uploads," but a subsequent maintainer-level code inspection in its discussion shows \`textures … MB\` includes bytes **read to hash** unchanged content even if no staging upload occurs; a synthetic WARP test read 16,384 bytes and staged **zero** on the second unchanged refresh. Do not sell the full reported number as saved PCIe bandwidth.
- **This fork already has** \`texture-digest\` [\`63b87e8\`](https://github.com/Zaxix/VIctory/commit/63b87e8ca5e26f9c20194ea576fd7d93281a25f3): XXH3-128 vs \`VICTORY_TEXTURE_DIGEST=blake3\`, with separate hashed/unchanged counters. On the Ryzen 5800X (Zen 3) a faster digest **may** reduce CPU time if texture hashing is the hot path; measure, don't assume the contributor's GB/s results apply.
- **Remap correctness:** upstream [#916](https://github.com/Marvisak/VIctory/issues/916) found a \`title_memory_remapped\` / \`UnmapViewOfFile2\` ordering race in an *epoch-cached* design, fixed there by notifying after unmap. **Do not copy that epoch fix blindly**: Claude's \`texture-digest\` commit explicitly says this fork does not cache the mapping epoch and instead uses mapping guards. Its specific race/correctness tests still need actual Windows execution.
- **If hashing remains dominant — next idea (hypothesis):** only cache a per-subresource digest with a **memory-map generation/epoch that is updated after completed remapping**, and do not skip the underlying read for untracked/possibly-DMA-written memory. First prototype separate counters for unchanged/changed, remap misses, digest CPU ms, actual detile and upload bytes. Partial detiling [#915](https://github.com/Marvisak/VIctory/issues/915) makes sense only if the dirty set is exact and page-to-tile overlap is correct. Tests: stable virtual address remapped to different physical backing, streaming mips appearing late, tail-byte mutation, concurrent unmap, failed upload retry. This is **not** an adaptive memory manager.

### R14 / P2 — Ray tracing has a new *working but much slower* implementation path

- **Verified public commit and source:** fxceless [\`9a9091d\`](https://github.com/fxceless/victory-fork/commit/9a9091d180f044726463d3db819808086daeebae), 2026-11-27. It restricts each list's memory synchronization to the copies its dispatches reference, records **64 KiB GPU-written pages** instead of treating every remembered buffer as written, and reads a ray-structure SRV address from \`RaytracingAccelerationStructure.Location\` instead of defaulting to zero. It also adjusts instance/procedural node return semantics. Reported result: previous builder null write was the **title's 30-second watchdog** because no frame completed; after fixes shadows and bounce light appear but execution falls to **0.2–3 FPS**, with about **8 GB of host-copy synchronization per frame** in that contributor's trace.
- **Critical source warning:** \`memory.rs\` admits a list may miss a copy reached only through an indirect pointer from another list; \`Shadow::pull_back\` admits that a title CPU write made after the GPU write can be overwritten because the destination was not snapshotted. This is not yet a correctness-complete cross-platform memory protocol. Its local tests and reports do not establish correctness on your fork or your Ryzen PC.
- **Proposed test-first improvement:** trace a **per-submission list of guest memory ranges actually accessed**, and distinguish read, write, pointer-chased unknown and unmapped. For uncertain pointer-chased access, conservatively retain the old path. For proven writes, track 64 KiB dirty pages only; copy them back **after the producing queue fence** and protect against CPU writes with a per-range version or pre-image comparison (or serialize the overlapping ownership). Before moving work off the frame path, prove the texture/readback/RT results match a strict baseline. Do not pretend the observed BVH node format alone is native PC DXR, and do not borrow a game-specific offset without validating the binary SHA.
- **Cross-reference:** the experiment is compatible with *researching* the GPU access-flag counters in Zaxix branch \`gpu-access-costs\`, but that module says it **does not currently skip any work** and cannot observe every \`VirtualFree\`; never turn a read-only flag into an unchecked no-copy shortcut. The owner's **adaptive memory manager stays deferred**.
- **Priority rationale:** ray-traced output is valuable, but a path at 0.2–3 FPS is not an unconditional replacement for a stable, faster build.

### R15 / P2 — Scoped barriers and host queries: delay until the hazard graph is known

- **Confirmed upstream work:** [PR #3138](https://github.com/Marvisak/VIctory/pull/3138) implements real GPU queries and fixes a timestamp-overflow bug via 128-bit scaling. The fork's \`VICTORY_OCCLUSION\` experiment should be measured as a separate feature: validate counts and waits before calling it a culling gain.
- **Barrier proposal (unimplemented design):** log for each resource/subresource: guest operation, host queue, last writer, next read/write, required state/layout, barrier type, and queue-fence dependency. Only then replace a global barrier with precise resource/subresource barriers; preserve UAV ordering and aliasing dependencies. Cross-queue \`Wait\`/\`Signal\` must still establish the correct happens-before relation. Compare old/new output with GPU-based validation and debug captures on the exact card. A smaller count of barriers by itself is not a win.
- **Official references:** [D3D12 enhanced barriers](https://microsoft.github.io/DirectX-Specs/d3d/D3D12EnhancedBarriers.html), [Multi-engine synchronization](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/direct3d12/user-mode-heap-synchronization). This design is a hypothesis, **not a verified patch**.

**Before using any listed switch:** verify it exists on the *checked-out target branch*; experimental features may be absent from \`main\`. Full DRED, GPU validation and per-command timing can perturb the measured workload; run them as separate diagnostic passes from release A/Bs. Count frame duration for the same drawn scene/draw-call range, not menus/fades/loading. If a run never reaches a usable steady scene, record that as a blocker rather than invent a number.


## P1/P2 — Adaptive memory placement (owner reactivated 2026-11-27)

**Objective:** make VIctory's CPU-canonical Xbox guest RAM and GPU-local host copies act like coherent unified memory **without** blindly placing all title buffers in RTX 4070 VRAM. The host RTX 4070 has 12 GB physical VRAM, but the **actual per-process DXGI Budget varies**. This is a new research-and-design track, not a claim a fix already exists. Do not create branches, commits, code edits, or PRs in this research checklist.

- [ ] **R23 — Adaptive-memory baseline and branch identity (P1).** Determine the actual Claude target branch and commit; compare it to \`main\`, \`gpu-access-costs\` (commit [\`259dc726\`](https://github.com/Zaxix/VIctory/commit/259dc7261ad790794d4349b3fccd248092fe7c54)), and \`spread-lean-nvidia\`. The experimental \`shadow.rs\` already has \`VICTORY_CARD_BUFFERS\`, \`card_limits()\` with a 1 MiB/1,024 MiB default, \`CARD_REFUSED\`, \`CARD_STATS\`, and \`card_room_kept()\`; **this is a fixed-size placement tier, not an adaptive runtime algorithm**. \`gpu_access.rs\` counts reads/writes by Xbox \`PAGE_GRAPHICS_*\` flags and explicitly does **not** use these flags to skip work because \`VirtualFree\` can leave stale entries. Instrument, do not reimplement, until confirmed missing on target. Measure the host's DXGI local Budget/CurrentUsage, non-local use, resource residency, copies/twins/marks, GPU bandwidth, CPU prepare, copy-back, fence waits, and 30–60 min trajectory. **Done when:** cross-branch feature-presence matrix + representative per-frame resource-cost ledger exists.
- [ ] **R24 — Prove access semantics, alias identity and coherency (P1).** Correlate guest \`XMemVirtualAlloc\` permissions with *actual* SRV/UAV/alias access, computed-address and bindless writes, and CPU writes during GPU copy-back. Identify full allocation and *mapping generation*, including same-VA remaps, title frees via APIs not currently observed, and descriptors already submitted. Mark uncertain/unknown memory as conservatively writable and do not turn read-only flags into skipped copy-back until tests support it. Include \`shadow::copy_back\`, write-watch, remap guard, DirectStorage and GPU page marks. **Done when:** per-range lifetime/write ledger, no unexplained writes to declared read-only regions, and deterministic concurrent write/fence tests.
- [ ] **R25 — Design a minimal, opt-in GPU-local admission/eviction algorithm (P2).** Compare \`system-copy\`, \`GPU_UPLOAD\` (if *observed supported*), \`device-local+staging mirror\`, and \`in-place\` **only where safe and explicitly tested**. Estimate measured GPU time saved versus CPU refresh/copy-back, PCIe traffic, allocator cost, snapshot/twin VRAM and queue stalls; don't rank candidates only by size or draw count. Prefer predictive/admission decisions at allocation boundaries first, hysteresis to avoid flip-flops, fail-open fallback on budget pressure, never evict in-flight resources. Keep GPU-local written-page marks in consideration but note that #2465 was scene-neutral/slightly slower on RTX 5090 even though its microbench improved. **Done when:** policy specification with conservative unknown classification, safety gates and independent knobs exists; **no patch until Claude has baseline data**.
- [ ] **R26 — Integrate safely with guest-VA stability and GPU fences (P2).** State machine must never change the guest address, invalidate mapped pages mid-use, release referenced heaps early, overwrite newer CPU data during GPU readback, or reuse a resource whose descriptor is still in flight. A migration must wait for producers, reconcile dirty CPU/GPU pages, publish new host view/descriptor generation to *future* lists only, and retire old copies after their last queue fence. Host \`OpenExistingHeapFromAddress*\` is risky on Windows (pins/free/protect issues and same-address reuse); it is **not** the default discrete-NVIDIA solution. **Done when:** adversarial tests cover remap, concurrent CPU/GPU stores, unknown pointer-chase writes, DirectStorage writes, device loss, DXGI budget shrink, descriptor lifetime and 64 KiB boundary cases.
- [ ] **R27 — RTX 4070 controlled A/B and rollback (P2).** Against the exact target branch, compare existing fixed tiers (\`VICTORY_CARD_BUFFERS_MIN_MB=1\`, \`VICTORY_CARD_BUFFERS_MB=1024\` where already implemented) with a *telemetry-only* scorer, then opt-in adaptive placement if a validated implementation exists. Run reproducible Series X scenes at stable draws, cold vs warmed caches, then 30–60+ min with VRAM pressure. Record median/p95 wall frame ms, CPU prep, GPU time, copied/hashed/detiled bytes, copy-back stalls, peak budgets and rendering/corruption counters. **Done when:** improvement is reproducible on RTX 4070 without device removal, visual regressions or budget thrashing; otherwise default off and retain logs.

**Cross-referenced evidence and known traps:**
- [#3102](https://github.com/Marvisak/VIctory/pull/3102) already prototypes host detection, video-memory mirrors, GPU upload heap and in-place self-tests; its RTX 5070 Ti synthetic benchmark reports 58.1 -> 29.0 ms/submission (not a title FPS number). It explicitly records a remaining **CPU write vs GPU copy-back race**, failed-copy fallback, writable mirrors, and **system-RAM-resident written-page bitmaps**. Do not cherry-pick wholesale (20 files, ~5,425 lines reported in review).
- [#2112](https://github.com/Marvisak/VIctory/pull/2112) shows that true in-place can reduce copies but pin sparse allocations, stall Windows \`VirtualFree/VirtualProtect\`, produce skinning artefacts, and reuse stale same-VA pages without generation IDs. Its Windows/NVIDIA regressions rule out treating zero-copy as the universal objective.
- [Belobug #444](https://github.com/Belobug/VIctory-Belobug-AMD/pull/444) reports a 12 GB NVIDIA fixed-tier test: 16 MiB/512 MiB -> 1 MiB/1,024 MiB reduced CPU snapshot 25.6 -> 3.1 ms and preparation 87 -> 51 ms with median 4.9 -> 8.8 FPS **on one contributor run**; your experimental code already documents the 1 MiB/1,024 MiB defaults. No claim of those numbers on the owner's RTX 4070.
- [#2175](https://github.com/Marvisak/VIctory/pull/2175) and [#2465](https://github.com/Marvisak/VIctory/pull/2465) moved GPU-written mark bitmaps to VRAM. The #2465 title test on RTX 5090 did not show a robust overall improvement; scheduling extra per-list readback/clear can erase atomics gains. [#2958](https://github.com/Marvisak/VIctory/pull/2958) measured wave-coalesced marks on RTX 4070 SUPER; do not assume an RTX 4070 gain.
- [fxceless 9a9091d](https://github.com/fxceless/victory-fork/commit/9a9091d180f044726463d3db819808086daeebae) gets visible ray-traced shadows with list-scoped memory syncing and 64 KiB written pages, but reported 0.2–3 FPS and unresolved pointer-chase / CPU-write conflicts. Its high sync traffic is a workload for telemetry, not a reason to skip coherency.
- **Official API checks:** [DXGI budget/usage](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/dxgi1_4/ns-dxgi1_4-dxgi_query_video_memory_info), [D3D12 residency](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/direct3d12/residency), [EnqueueMakeResident](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/d3d12/nf-d3d12-id3d12device3-enqueuemakeresident), [D3D12 GPU upload heaps](https://microsoft.github.io/DirectX-Specs/d3d/D3D12GPUUploadHeaps.html). VRAM capacity **does not equal** the current WDDM process budget; a GPU upload heap is *not* proof copies will remain fast when over budget.

**Handoff for Claude:** the companion local TXT \`VIctory_Zaxix_Adaptive_Memory_Manager_RTX4070_Claude_Research_2026-11-27.txt\` contains the deeper engineering proposal, risks, proposed state transitions, test vectors, and source links. User wants an actual manager eventually; still require profiling-first and an opt-in/rollback safety path.


## P1/P2 — Independent pipeline-cache research while adaptive memory work proceeds

These are **separate from the adaptive memory manager** and can be investigated without modifying \`shadow.rs\`, residency policy or descriptor lifetime logic. The following are source-level findings in integrated \`main\`, not verified device-loss causes.

- [ ] **R28 — P1: pipeline cache publication and retry correctness.** \`crates/victory-gpu/src/pipeline/library.rs\` still saves library data using \`host-pipelines.tmp\`; \`save(now)\` releases its scheduling lock before the long serialization/write/rename finishes. Overlapping saves can target the same filename, and \`library.added.store(0,...)\` happens *before* successful \`replace_library\` publication. Distinct from the earlier fixed pipeline-library mapped-handle lifetime issue. Compare [upstream PR #1660](https://github.com/Marvisak/VIctory/pull/1660) (unique per-save temp paths + atomic publishing) and the failure-retention/single-publisher review improvements in [PR #1653](https://github.com/Marvisak/VIctory/pull/1653). Test concurrent periodic + shutdown saves; injected serialization/rename/power-failure; ensure no lost dirty count or last-writer-wins regression, and both old and new cache files remain usable. **Acceptance:** reproducible unit/fault-injection tests and Windows run; no corruption or unrecoverable cache loss. **Claude owns any code changes.**
- [ ] **R29 — P2: pipeline-library sharding only after R28.** Integrated \`main\` already uses mapped files, keeps the original file handle open, permits independent pipeline-name loads, and saves only when pipelines changed; avoid reimplementing these. It still serializes *the entire* host PSO library whenever new pipelines are stored. [Upstream PR #1653](https://github.com/Marvisak/VIctory/pull/1653) proposes 16 deterministic shards and publishing only dirty shards, but its own control found **all 16 dirty shards slower** than one blob and its head includes critical persistence fixes. Compare a no-shard control versus sharded saves at 1/4/16 dirty shards and incremental real loads, record save time, peak RAM, IO bytes, first-run startup cost and number of dropped draws. **Acceptance:** measurable benefit on current hardware without new cache failure or device removal.


## P0–P2 — Additional hardware research R30–R34 (2026-11-27)

**Owner's target:** RTX 4070 12 GB; Ryzen 7 5800X (8 cores / 16 threads); 64 GB DDR4-3600. **Research only:** Claude handles all source and Git changes; update this checklist without deleting Claude's work. The current combined `main` already includes the earlier optimisation branches. Cross-reference existing branch features before proposing new edits. These tasks can be researched separately while Claude implements adaptive placement (R23–R27).

- [ ] **R30 — BCPack decoder throughput and streaming queue stalls (P1).** `crates/victory-hle/src/modules/dstorage.rs` records about 12 MB/s/worker in one earlier test (run 327), and its DirectStorage status entries are ordered behind earlier requests on the same queue. `crates/victory-formats/src/bcpack.rs` implements 12/16 interleaved rANS lanes serially and explicitly suggests separating input per lane before independent lane decoding. The BC block reconstruction depends on prior blocks via the history dictionary, which limits parallelism even if entropy decoding speeds up. Time storage reads, zlib inflation, rANS, BC1/3/4/5/6/7 reconstruction, destination write and status delay separately. Build verified byte-for-byte CPU baselines, then profile SIMD, avoiding unnecessary allocation, per-lane decode and queue-aware scheduling. GPU compute must be **custom BCPack**, not Windows DirectStorage GDeflate; include upload, fence and readback cost in comparisons. **Gate:** deterministic valid/malformed/edge-stream parity, unchanged queue ordering/priority and matched world texture captures on RTX 4070; no claimed FPS until tested.
- [ ] **R31 — Thread oversubscription and guest affinity (P1).** Source: `dstorage::scheduler` starts one worker per `available_parallelism()` (usually 16 on this Ryzen); `pipeline::make::workers` uses logical-count-minus-4 clamped 4–14 (usually 12). Shadow parallel copies may use up to 6 jobs with `VICTORY_KEPT_THREADS` or scoped threads, plus host submit, relay, heap, async readbacks, guest threads and driver workers. Count *active runnable* threads per phase, not merely created thread totals. Use WPR/WPA context switches, ready time, CPU use (Precise), blocking, staging starvation, pipeline queue lengths and draw drops. Audit whether Xbox affinity-related functions are imported and implemented; do not assume `XSetThreadProcessor` exists without import/source confirmation. Proposed isolated A/B worker limits: DirectStorage {4,8,12,16}, pipeline {2,4,8,12}; start with idle-vs-streaming distinctions and measure side effects. `VICTORY_DISK_CORES=slow` targets hybrid CPUs and is not a Zen 3 fix. **Gate:** same scene, output, queue semantics, no starvation or priority inversion and measured p95 frame-time gain.
- [ ] **R32 — NVIDIA driver/Windows benchmark manifest and ReBAR check (P0).** Record OS build, GPU driver, WDDM, actual `D3D12_FEATURE_DATA_D3D12_OPTIONS16::GPUUploadHeapSupported` value, DXGI local budget, ReBAR from hardware tools, HAGS, Game Mode, NVIDIA power settings, shader cache size, SSD free space, RAM speed, monitor refresh, display/VRR, resolution and shader-cache warmth. The NVIDIA game-profile/ReBAR optimisation whitelist is **not the same as D3D12 GPU upload heap capability**. Compare `VICTORY_WINDOW_UPLOAD=1` vs off only after confirming it is supported and use exactly the same build, driver and scene; both runs need GPU time, CPU prep, VRAM/copy traffic and correctness. NVIDIA driver cache size and the VIctory host pipeline library are **separate caches**, not one shared size limit. **Gate:** saved manifest and repeatable A/B, no unsupported assumption of FPS improvement from HAGS/Game Mode/power-profile changes.
- [ ] **R33 — First-run shader cache and pack-aware pipeline prebuild (P2).** Verified CLI on integrated `main`: `apps/victory/src/main.rs` supports `translate --warm <file>` and `translate --warm all`, plus a separate `translate --pipelines` inspection mode; `translate::warm` translates into the run's shader cache, no host PSOs. Upstream `Marvisak/VIctory` provides `translate --cache --pipelines` and `apps/victory/src/prebuild.rs` that reconstructs title shader-pack pipelines for the host pipeline library; this piece is absent from the current fork. First try the existing warm-all path under the correct `VICTORY_HOST_WAVE`/`VICTORY_VIEW_LIMIT` settings, then audit how much of actual host PSO preparation remains. Compare count of translated programs, cache hits, PSOs built/stored, queued/drop draws and first-world time cold vs warm. **Gate:** cache fingerprint compatibility, correct dynamic pipeline variants, safe resume after interrupted prebuild, no incorrect PSO reuse after driver/translator changes.
- [ ] **R34 — Frame pacing, relay and responsiveness (P2).** `crates/victory-gpu/src/frame.rs` implements a title frame interval with absolute ticks; scheduled event offsets are simplified, `VICTORY_LATE_WAITS=1` restores previous sleep-to-next-tick behaviour. The relay can send presents before CPU-side reconciliation. Measure actual display-present intervals (PresentMon/ETW), p50/p95/p99, GPU busy, render latency, queue depth, repeat/drop frames, title frame tokens and input-to-present proxy latency. Keep guest 30-fps semantic timing distinct from the current 5–10-fps rendering capability. Do not claim pacing produces more computed frames: compare regularity and latency at the same GPU workload and avoid waits that defer input. **Gate:** charts of variance/latency in controlled world scenes, no dropped/duplicated present or broken frame token.
