Shader binaries/game archives never added to GitHub.



### R58 — Run12 real-game shader follow-up: exact guest-address DWORD consumers; two paired Wave64 emissions (research-only, 2026-11-27)

> **Owner input:** Run12 `run12.zip` (41,151 lines, 2026-11-27 14:41:03–14:55:50 UTC), `gpu12.csv` (859 samples), pasted excerpts, previous Run11 logs and R57 original shader binary study. Owner closed the window at 14:55:49 UTC. **No observed device removed/hung, panic or driver error**; the normal exit suspend timeout is not a crash. The run still used FAST, levels-on-demand and GPU upload heaps; default paired-wave first attempts are now visibly logged. **No translator/driver fix applied or proven.** External game/shader bytes are private and must never be committed.

- [ ] **R58 / P0 exactly five shadow compute variants STILL fail** with *identical* `a descriptor read only as data` GFX10 PCs to Run11: `96ccb14ef981fd85` Eighth `0x1e70`, `0e64f14fae060d04` Quarter `0x1e18`, `002e93a6cc2aa355` Half `0x1d70`, `07c93bfb77fecebb` Full `0x2070` (native `image_bvh64_intersect_ray`), `dbdbf36298727135` FullAdaptive `0x1e48`. The Run12 timestamped warnings are 14:41:11–12, **before** story archive load; a failed first translation is not evidence that the shader dispatched. [R57](#r57--original-game-shader-binary-forensics-exact-five-shadow-variants-resourceheap-guest-bvh-address-and-wave64-verified-corpus-research-only-2026-11-27) verified original containers and `RootType::ResourceHeap` native `layout[228]`, `user_data s6`.
- [ ] **R58 / P0 the new, decisive `DESC-TRACE` liveness pattern** confirms at each exact dynamic `s_load_dwordx4` from `s6:s7 + computed offset` the **first two output DWORDs are live as guest address bits, last two read by nothing at those load sites**: Eighth `0x1de8 s36:s39` (d0/d1, d2/d3 dead), Quarter `0x1d90 s32:s35`, Half `0x1ce8 s24:s27`, Full `0x1d44 s0:s3`, FullAdaptive `0x1dc0 s16:s19`. Four use high-DWORD `&0xffff` plus `s_lshr_b64` by 8; Full masks `0xffffff00` low, `0xff/0xffff` high and also shifts by 8. The **error PC is downstream**, not necessarily the load; instrument `Address{param,offset,dynamic}` and heap slot at origin. Treat the liveness statement as load-site specific, not proof the rest of a shader never needs record count/stride/format.
- [ ] **R58 / P0 exact two-DWORD dynamic guest-VA metadata prototype:** [`emit/lower.rs::view_address`](../crates/victory-shader/src/emit/lower.rs) only allows fixed `RootType::Table`, `a.dynamic.is_none()`, 32-byte aligned and declared 4-DWORD SRV/UAV. These five load from dynamic `RootType::ResourceHeap`, so the current `descriptor_data` fallback cannot emit their **guest VA**. Give the shader access to **guest low32 and guest high16** keyed by the true dynamically selected view slot/alias, preserving a separate D3D12 handle. The virtualized `vVIE` tag/generation is NOT a raw RDNA2 descriptor. Derive AS SRV address from description `Location` when `pResource=NULL` (upstream [#2098](https://github.com/Marvisak/VIctory/pull/2098)); avoid a zero `buffer_range` result. Coordinate dynamic tables ([#1906](https://github.com/Marvisak/VIctory/pull/1906)), capped NVIDIA 1,000,000 slots, mirror/copy/update, lifetime/fences, remapping and shader-cache fingerprint. At most 2 DWORDs per slot for this proven path; broad 4-DWORD rebuild ([#2363](https://github.com/Marvisak/VIctory/pull/2363)) not a verified fix for the 5 compute (published compute total unchanged), and not necessarily stride/format exact.
- [ ] **R58 / P0 incremental translation gates**: (A) print root provenance/heap index and bound View kind at each of 5 source PCs; (B) show guest d0/d1 match original view address for buffer and ray-tracing-AS `Location`; (C) offline 5/5 output or next distinct refusal, no new regressions; (D) dxil.dll validation; (E) host PSOs for four guest-Wave32 variants; (F) Full guest-Wave64 treated separately, do not force 64 lanes on RTX4070; (G) actual dispatch + GPU ray result readbacks vs independent reference; check 40/48-bit mask, carry and alias, null and stale views. PSO acceptance alone is **not** shader correctness.
- [ ] **R58 / P0 paired Wave64 actual improvement, limited scope**: Run12 selects **all 28** Run11 refused hashes by default. **Two successfully translated with paired Wave64 and were NOT refused by host pipeline creation:** `ffa3a7645374474b`, `98fef92f7a6d825e` (both have `host pipeline shaders=[compute …]` lines). **Neither has a `first bind` event** in Run12: program execution and visual correctness **not demonstrated**. The remaining **26** report paired refusal then fall back to WaveSize64 and are rejected by RTX4070. Partition: **16** LDS operation unsupported in pairs, **2** compute shared-memory budget exceeded, **1** unavailable group rendezvous, **7** unconfirmed FP32 mode. Do not treat 28→26 as 2 newly rendered passes without first-bind/dispatch.

First bind at frame **4101** (14:44:32.518 UTC). `VICTORY_WAVE_PAIRS_FP32=non-ieee-rne-ftz` is a potential **isolated** comparison only with guest initial/MODE proof, numerical golden waves, loop/partial EXEC and cross-half FP64 verification (see [#3502 adversarial test](https://github.com/Marvisak/VIctory/issues/3502#issuecomment-7218705676)); don't change all defaults.

The five shadow translation failures are not proven to be the builder-drop cause. Keep `VICTORY_BVH_BUILDER=0` for normal testing until separate earlier null-write crash resolved. Full BVH correctness needs builder + rays + valid initialized node data; test separately.

Closer `4500<=draws<5500` band Run11 N=9 median 183.2ms, Run12 N=71 median 159.7ms. These are **different scene histories, durations, cache states and workloads**; do NOT claim a controlled speedup or sum overlapping frame components. Run12 786 preparing-part reports: median watch/flight **30.6ms**, list steps **31.5**, flush **7.4**, `card_before` **14.95**; the card reports **92.2ms/frame idle/no work queued** at last sample (not necessarily all recoverable).

Final runtime LOCAL budget **10,590MiB** and **10,250MiB in use**, only ~262MiB free beside reserved; 250 hot stable PC copies needing 733MiB cannot all move to VRAM. ### R59 — Run12 VIDEO MEMORY, WORLD-ASSET STREAMING AND D3D12-vs-DX11 OPTIMIZATION RESEARCH (2026-11-27, research-only)

> **Scope/evidence:** owner requested VRAM optimization, loading buildings/models/assets, and whether a DX11/D3D12 translation can improve performance. Inspected current `resource.rs`, `tiles.rs`, `texture.rs`, `draw.rs`, `heap_worker.rs`, `shadow.rs`, `host.rs`, `dstorage.rs` through GitHub; parsed owner's Run12 full log into **eight time-series CSVs**; checked upstream independent reports and Microsoft Direct3D/DirectStorage docs. **No implementation, measured speedup or build is claimed by this R59 entry.** Game binaries, title memory and raw shaders stay off public GitHub. Full private handoff: `Defeat_R59_VRAM_Streaming_D3D12_Optimization_Claude_Handoff_2026-11-27.txt`; ZIP `Defeat_R59_VRAM_Streaming_D3D12_Research_Pack_2026-11-27.zip` (TXT+8 CSVs).

**Measured situation, Run12:**
- LOCAL budget/usage 162 approximately five-second reports: median worst observed usage **10,218 MiB**; median minimum budget **10,552.5 MiB**; last 10,314 / 10,527; window minimum of (minimum budget minus maximum use) **70 MiB**, median **320 MiB**, **51/162 windows under 256 MiB** and 130/162 under 512 MiB. Conservative mixed-extrema indicator, not simultaneous free bytes. Shared/NON_LOCAL usage is NOT dedicated GPU VRAM. This is a **budget-pressure** problem; globally admitting new copies past `resource::room` can cause paging/device removal.
- 153 sparse tile reports: median allocated level tiles **323 MiB**, last **197 MiB**; tile pool held **704 MiB** at last sample (11 x 64MiB heaps). Last **3,055 MiB theoretical unmapped mip memory** means allocation ALREADY avoided, not 3,055 MiB free-able allocated VRAM. Difference pool minus tile assignments (507 MiB) cannot necessarily be freed because live tiles can be scattered across partly populated heaps.
- 162 texture heap queue reports: last **7,550 queued slots**, only **203 currently due**, **2,016 waiting for mapped pages** and **4,266 partially uploaded**. These classes overlap / are not a partition. Last oldest due 1,573 frames. Event-driven mapped/unmapped/written notifications already re-due slots (6.8 million cumulative notifications and 41,766 cumulative slots made due; zero dropped notifications at last reporting sample). Distinguish missing data vs not-due vs VRAM block vs GPU/binding failure, rather than labeling all 7,550 as 'files not loading.'
- 784 heap worker reports: median **34.2ms/frame working**, median **0.4ms/frame detiling**, meaning optimize eligibility/resource creation/wait/synchronization **only after adding sub-phase timings**. 752 texture digest reports: median **17.6 decimal MB/frame hashed** in **1.25ms**, median changed 3.3MB. `host` 'textures MB/frame' counts **bytes read for checking as well as uploads**, so don't infer upload traffic from that counter (see upstream [#3269](https://github.com/Marvisak/VIctory/issues/3269)).
- 786 card-clock reports: median **76.1ms/frame no Defeat GPU work queued** (p90 210.1); of that idle, median **64.4ms/frame occurred while submitter thread was working**. These are distributions, not same-frame additive measurements. Prioritize CPU submission/copy-back critical path/queue starvation, **not API switch**.
- 145 DirectStorage five-second slow-request summaries: title staging **32MiB**, **16 worker threads**, slowest request median **35ms**, p90 289ms, max 913ms; summed over windows 420 non-BCPack +52 BCPack requests took over 250ms, none over 1s. 'Non-BCPack' may still be zlib and physical-page writes. Need phase timing (I/O, decode, page lock, retry, status head-of-line); do not blindly increase workers or conflate with disk transfer bandwidth.
- Last placement window: **4,161 PC copies +494 windows =2,082.9MiB** host shared-access; **303 GPU-upload windows=1,252.0MiB** (not necessarily dedicated LOCAL GPU memory); **97 LOCAL card copies=939.5MiB**. Last LOCAL available headroom cannot accommodate migrating all PC copies. Last frame builder disabled still dominates dropped draws (R58 558 of 561); missing buildings/models may be missing mesh/shader/culling/indirect outputs, **not necessarily stalled storage or missing VRAM**.

**Already implemented — do NOT reimplement or claim a new win merely by enabling:**
- `resource.rs::room` (~1486) queries DXGI LOCAL budget and includes tile allocations; current `VICTORY_TEXTURE_ROOM_PERCENT` **default=96**, not upstream old 90, and `targets_room_kept` default 2,000MiB. `VICTORY_TEXTURE_BUDGET_MB` overrides safety on LOCAL accounting: hazardous if treated as a force-all-textures fix.
- `resource.rs::let_go_idle` (~1825) already retires title-filled idle textures only after idle time, 4 frames and no in-flight list or descriptor references. `VICTORY_TEXTURE_SWEEP=0` disables this safety; leave default ON.
- `tiles.rs`: partial mips `CreateReservedResource`, `UpdateTileMappings`, clamped SRVs already exist. **Tiles are explicitly not taken back when title releases a mip while host texture remains alive**; `give_back` only reclaims runs when host copy is dropped; a 64MiB pool heap returns only when entirely free (and last stays). `VICTORY_TILES_SMALL_WHEN_FULL_MB` in current code defaults to **256**, despite stale comment implying 0. Low-mip fallback preserves blurred instead of black image under pressure.
- `texture.rs`: XXH3, write-watch, guarded view-memory and a fallback every-eighth-frame hash already exist; one partly mapped staging branch has a **30-frame minimum re-read**, but `draw.rs::State::heap_textures` also uses page-map notifications and fallback 8–64-frame due checks, so cannot assume ALL textures wait 30 frames. CPU detile is currently a small measured cost compared with copy/submit.
- `heap_worker.rs` is already asynchronous, holds resources and only publishes after associated upload list submits; lists flush at 128MiB or 80ms. `draw.rs::heap_textures` already has default 192MiB/250ms staging limits and 3x catchup with >=1024 due. Increases don't override resource::room.
- Console `dstorage.rs` is an **XCRDAPI compatibility implementation**, using `FileExt::seek_read`, zlib and BCPack software paths and weighted priorities. PC DirectStorage **GDeflate GPU mode is not an interchangeable BCPack decoder**, and original console physical-page and FIFO status semantics must survive.

**Implementation backlog, priority ordered and requiring Claude's synthetic tests before real-game A/B:**
- [ ] **R59/P0 scene provenance and asset-latency trace:** correlate request completion (file/offset, codec, queue age, physical-page mapped/written) -> live title memory -> descriptor view/texture or vertex/index buffer -> resource created -> GPU upload submit/fence -> PSO/draw/mesh/indirect output -> depth/G-buffer pixels. Expose per-stage histogram and *reason* missing assets. Distinguish absent geometry from textured-but-black geometry, and BVH builder from building mesh. Keep proprietary bytes/asset names and DXIL out of public logs.
- [ ] **R59/P0 CPU/GPU critical-path timings:** instrument `host::card_before`, copy-back/copy-written, readback waiting, queue2 handoff, texture-heap pass phase buckets, descriptor mirror/update, command list create/close and actual GPU queue timestamps (PIX in local short frame). Run12 queue starvation ~64ms while submitter busy is stronger evidence than D3D12 driver throughput alone. Prototype only fence-safe decoupling and GPU dirty-page change tracking; preserve immediately title-readable UAV outputs.
- [ ] **R59/P0 LOCAL budget policy:** per-class footprint + pending/retired-with-fence + last-used reporting (target/depth, title texture, tile pool, host card-buffer copy, GPU upload, transient readback, pipeline). Dynamic budget change notification/hysteresis and admission priority: critical RT/DS and sampled low mips first, speculative distant high mips later. Maintain headroom on NVIDIA ~10.5GiB effective budget; `ID3D12Device::Evict` is not equivalent to releasing live resources. <https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/direct3d12/residency>.
- [ ] **R59/P1 optional sparse mip-level reclaim:** first output per-heap free-run occupancy/hidden tiles and last GPU fence (no code behavior change), then safely reclaim no-longer-referenced level tiles *after* remaking SRVs and all relevant GPU queues' fences; if fragmentation is proved, compact by copying live tile contents, queue-ordered remapping and only release wholly free 64MiB heaps. Handle packed mip tail, aliasing and mixed copy/compute queues. **Do not claim 704−197=507MiB immediately reclaimable.**
- [ ] **R59/P1 opt-in sampled-only mip-drop:** independent [upstream fork issue #737](https://github.com/Marvisak/VIctory/issues/737) and [open PR #1272](https://github.com/Marvisak/VIctory/pull/1272) propose/verify `VICTORY_TEXTURE_MIP_DROP` on other branches, NOT Defeat; ideal ~75%/~93.75% per eligible 2D image after one/two highest mips, not whole-VRAM saving. Add opt-in behind tests for original console->host mip remap, every SRV dimensional window, staging swizzle, CopyResource/CopyTextureRegion/resolve and descriptor generations. NEVER drop RT/DS/UAV/BVH/AS or textures whose mutability/alias makes it unsafe. Account demand mips already unmapped; benchmark character/building image quality.
- [ ] **R59/P1 DStorage CPU scheduling AB**: 16 vs 12 vs 8 `VICTORY_DSTORAGE_WORKERS` (one independent switch per run), plus request read/decode/page-write/PHYSICAL-lock timings and FIFO status backlog. Upstream [#1633](https://github.com/Marvisak/VIctory/issues/1633) reports +28% on a different host after a physical mapping lock change: NOT proven to apply to this fork. Avoid uncontrolled 32MiB staging increase and per-request logging overhead.
- [ ] **R59/P2 buffer placement and CPU-visible arena**: priority by measured GPU reads + CPU rewrite rate. Existing 939.5MiB card copies/1,252MiB GPU upload windows consume/affect different budgets; no blanket LOCAL promotion with 70–320MiB headroom. [Upstream #407](https://github.com/Marvisak/VIctory/issues/407) suggests imported shared memory and translated GPU VAs but PCIe latency and import capability vary; bit-exact probes and opt-in per-allocation only.
- [ ] **R59/P2 API choice:** **Keep D3D12/DXIL**, not DX11 port. DX11 does not change RTX4070's 32-lane hardware width and lacks a drop-in Shader Model 6/wave/mesh/bindless/ray/BVH path; reimplementation may worsen driver synchronization. D3D12 targeted tests: PIX queue and GPU timing, PSO cache segmentation only if compile saving measured, redundant barrier elimination after graphics debug validation, descriptor writes/heap cap and retirement, optional enhanced barriers if supported. Deferred Vulkan alternative only after portable IR + controlled baseline; not a short-run optimization. Microsoft: <https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/direct3d12/porting-from-direct3d-11-to-direct3d-12> and <https://microsoft.github.io/DirectX-Specs/d3d/D3D12EnhancedBarriers.html>.
- [ ] **R59 acceptance gates**: (1) no game bytes in repo; (2) synthetic exact texture buffer/page/descriptor output incl aliases and remaps; (3) multi-queue fence lifetime, stale dynamic-slot tests and no DRED violations; (4) per-scene cold-load-to-first-visible-building timings and clear root-cause classification; (5) 3 matched release runs baseline/candidate (same game time/draw counts, warmed vs cold shader cache reported) compare p50/p90 frametime/LOCAL peaks/queue-missing/waiting/mip visual quality; (6) no crash/driver removal with long streaming and clean game close. Avoid claims from upstream unrelated hardware or changed scene loads.

At the **last logged power-of-two checkpoint 23:07:33.714**, **1,048,576** eligible stale 64KiB pages were narrowed to their **6,783,106** written 4KiB pieces: **27,783.6 decimal MB** candidate copy volume instead of **68,719.5 decimal MB** whole-page equivalent (difference **40,935.9 decimal MB**). **1,688,280 other stale pages still copied whole** (unknown/old/non-kernel changes). This is cumulative for the *eligible path* at a checkpoint, **NOT VRAM freed, a verified PCIe saving, or a controlled FPS gain**; no logged final cumulative snapshot after this threshold.

More restricted `4500<=draws<5500` Run12 N=71 **159.7ms**, Run13 N=13 **207.5ms** (late Run13 N=6 **187.9ms**). **Not a controlled regression or optimization result**: shader-cache cold rebuild/failures, differing scenes, renderer work and background load confound all pairs. `0.0ms on GPU` frame-log component means *CPU waited 0ms for GPU*, NOT GPU performed no work.
- **Gameplay-aligned telemetry (<=2s nearest 5s frames, NOT exact single-frame sync):** Run12 N=122 vs Run13 N=45, late Run13 N=15. Median `GPU queue empty` **81.9 vs 137.4ms** (late **117.3**); `GPU idle while submitter busy` **69.3 vs 94.2ms** (late **78.4**); card-copy `landing` **61.9 vs 82.1ms** (late **83.5**); write-watch and under-flight **32.8 vs 20.2ms** (late **18.2**); list steps **33.4 vs 49.5ms** (late **35.9**). Run13 *all-scene late* medians ~31.8ms GPU idle and 1.9ms write-watch largely mix in low-draw menu windows: **do not use those to describe the gameplay bottleneck**. Times overlap and MUST NOT be added into a frame estimate.
- **Final sparse tile report, Run12→Run13:** alive **1403→1517**, levels actually given tiles **197→200MiB**, pool allocated D3D12 heaps **704→512MiB**, full all-level counterfactual **3252→3425MiB**, **levels NOT allocated** **3055→3225MiB**. ### R63 — Latest main-source checkpoint: what to focus on after Run15 (2026-11-28, research-only)

> **Why this section exists:** owner requested a new GitHub review with FPS as the primary goal. Repository commits checked as of this review: `5d4a13f78fc93d8ada7ebf658fb51694841b2394` current main documentation HEAD, and **`25462507841c5ae2d7f3a0c35e0914d074b5b77a` latest source change**. Checked the README owner-run table, `crates/victory-gpu/src/plaincopy.rs`, `shadow.rs`, `inplace.rs`, `watch.rs`, `fast.rs` and `crates/victory-hle/src/modules/dstorage.rs`. **No Run16 log or fresh post-2546250 FPS benchmark found in this GitHub snapshot.** This is source and backlog verification, not a new run or a newly measured improvement.

**Current order — exactly what is already coded versus what needs evidence:**
- [ ] **P0 Run16 release-build verification of 2546250 (highest immediate ROI).** `VICTORY_WRITE_PLAIN` is already **ON by default under `VICTORY_FAST`** (`plaincopy.rs::store_on`); `=0` reverts to `NtWriteVirtualMemory`, `=alt` switches each 20s. Claude's AFTER-Run15 code implements `plaincopy::store_runs` to batch word runs, `shadow::write_merged_counted` and other write sites to count/access-check once per span, and `inplace::write_own` unlocks before copying. In Run15 prior build, on-vs-off separated copy-back significantly but **frame p50 improvement after transition guards was small**. First establish `2546250` builds and keeps **byte-identical game-visible data and correct hair/geometry**, then measure fixed `=1` with stable warmed route, optionally compare fixed `=0` without other knobs. Do NOT reimplement existing fast path.
- [ ] **P0 locate real remaining CPU serialization using new source counters.** `watch.rs::guards()` now collects caller-line `Mutex<Guards>` wait and hold time, printed by `guards_line`. **These are totals across ALL THREADS, not per-frame wall time**. In the next run, rank caller lines by `held` and `waited`, correlate to title thread ready/runnable state, actual copy-back, DStorage worker backlog, GPU command queue empty and frame p50/p90. Determine whether `writing`/page-remap/guard-protection has long *hold* or *waiting*; make one small exact-order/fence-safe edit only if a measured critical-path lock is identified. A source comment reports 200–450ms physical-page notification episodes in prior Run15; that is not a proven mutex wait distribution until the new report is run.
- [ ] **P0 unblock world-asset loading through worker/guard timing, not blind disk/decompression rewrite.** In `dstorage.rs::Lanes::count`, the `slowest_parts` max-update bug was **already repaired in 2546250**; new report should show nonzero slowest phase values. Keep queue wait `job.waited` distinct from `execute` time; conditional averages from >=250ms requests must not be presented as all-request latency. Trace worker availability, guest physical-page write notifications, lock wait vs hold, queue priority/FIFO completion and true asset-to-first-visible-mesh latency. Only controlled worker-count A/B after cause confirmed; `VICTORY_DSTORAGE_WORKERS` already configurable, default 16 on this host.
- [ ] **P0 synthetic correctness gate on the new optimization (no known bug claimed).** `store_runs` can have some guarded direct stores succeed before one fails, after which callers repeat the full run list through the kernel. Check remap/protection fault halfway through a batch, same VA reused for a different physical map, simultaneous title CPU overwrite between first store and kernel retry, adjacent 4KiB/64KiB boundaries and write-watch notifications. `inplace::write_own` now releases an own-view lock before actual stores; prove overlapping write order/fence generation under concurrent merges. Only enable as fast default if the specific guest-visible semantics are correct. These are *test hypotheses*, not established corruption.
- [ ] **P1 keep `VICTORY_DIRTY_4K=0` as an explicit image-stability gate**, because Claude's README reports blocky hair in Runs13/14 when on and their absence in Run15 when off; correlation, not proven cause. No blanket VRAM reservation override or extra mip-memory "savings": sparse levels already avoid unmapped allocations and local budget is tight. Defer expensive tile pool compaction until measured hidden tile/fence fragmentation.
- [ ] **P2 hold separate BVH/shader and DX11-port work while targeting FPS.** Four named shadow variants create PSOs without first binds, one Adaptive mask-as-data translator refusal, 26 WaveSize64 host PSO rejects and builder intentionally OFF. They remain crucial for eventual correct GI/visibility, not the next **measured** FPS patch. D3D12 is appropriate; fix CPU starvation first.

**Performance target and decision rule:** 5FPS is ~200ms/frame and 10FPS ~100ms/frame. Run15's `WRITE_PLAIN` in-run copy-back p50 ~66ms ON versus ~97ms OFF, but the transition-guarded full-frame p50 was only ~204ms ON vs ~207ms OFF. Do not extrapolate a 30ms copy-back reduction into a guaranteed 30ms total frame reduction. Next measurement should report matched >3000 draw and 4500–5500 draw windows, p50/p90 frame ms, CPU critical-path timeline, GPU card no-work time, staged/worker queue-wait distribution, no DRED and screenshots. **No source or Claude-owned status changed in this R63 research entry.**




### R64 — Between-game-run FPS development batch and cross-emulator technique audit (historical proposal; progress superseded by R65, 2026-11-28)

> **Owner direction:** avoid needing a 10–15-minute game run for every tweak; use substantial Windows/source-only progress between a few high-value A/B gameplay runs. Also **routinely compare active emulators** and exploit legitimate, proven techniques not already implemented by Defeat. **This is research planning only** (not a claim anything below has passed tests or increased FPS). At audit start, latest Defeat source had Claude's post-Run15 changes [2546250](https://github.com/Zaxix/Defeat/commit/25462507841c5ae2d7f3a0c35e0914d074b5b77a), with the later checklist [ddc5e3f](https://github.com/Zaxix/Defeat/commit/ddc5e3faca2aca890f28b478c7ccdd2844de4177). Revalidate SHA before implementation. **All guest/game original data, raw logs and proprietary shader binaries stay private.**

**Development cadence:** `research/source audit → pure logic property tests → Windows synthetic/host-GPU tests (where required) → CPU microbenchmark on real 5800X, release build → 1–3 small optimizations with independent toggles and a full rollback → one matching gameplay Run16`. Synthetic passes are evidence of exactness or micro-CPU speed only, **not of FPS**; if a change affects GPU queues, descriptors or actual guest memory, at least one real Windows GPU test and a later game smoke/quality check are mandatory. Avoid pooling unrelated defaults into one undocumented fast switch; keep a candidate-by-candidate before/after record. Between-run batch ideally 3–5 focused PR/commits, **not** a broad refactor.

**HISTORICAL R64 proposed tasks — several are NOW IMPLEMENTED in 0c62860/490577d. Use the R65 status audit, NOT these unchecked historical task labels, for the live work queue:**
- [ ] **R64/A — Synthetic title write-back, merge and alias *safety* suite (P0, no game required).** In `victory-gpu/src/plaincopy.rs::store_runs` / `shadow.rs::{word_runs,write_merged_counted}` / `inplace.rs::write_own`, construct memory snapshots and compare to reference copy through the original kernel path. Cases: 4-byte word at unaligned starting address; writes straddling **4KiB** and **64KiB** boundaries; contiguous or >50k disjoint word runs; an unmapped/not-writable page halfway through a batch; direct partial success then complete kernel fallback; guest CPU write between original read and GPU merge; two titles views aliasing the same physical page; view-remap/generation change; concurrent merges/own-view writes and fence retirement. Use deterministic seeds and fault injection, assert entire title-visible byte result, not just return code. This extends existing `plaincopy.rs` **read-copy** fault tests and `dstorage.rs` physical-page tests—do not duplicate existing tests. No performance claim until measured.
- [ ] **R64/B — Exact small-write merge microbenchmark (P0/P1, no game).** Run15 reported **~54,000 changed-word runs/frame** and ~130k in-place stores/frame. Benchmark current `word_runs` + temporary `Vec<(offset,len)>` construction, `store_runs` batch and `gpu_access::note_gpu_writes` on synthetic 4KiB/64KiB stretches, sparse/dense/bursty distributions, 1–16 writer threads and same title-memory accessibility. Compare `allocations/write`, CPU time, cache misses, mutex wait/hold and bytes; use byte-identical golden output. Potential candidates **only if measured**: reuse per-thread run-vector capacity, stack-inline small-run storage, coalesce within *exact original 4-byte-word semantics*, or reduce avoidable atomics. *Never* combine across independent alias remaps or CPU-vs-GPU observable ordering. Accept candidate with meaningful CPU microbench improvement and no regression across distributions; log independent opt-out. New source already batches runs once—do not reimplement batching.
- [ ] **R64/C — Write-watch guard-lock simulator/property tests (P0, no game).** `watch.rs::guards` now measures lock wait/hold by `watch.rs` call line. Offline memory model of protected/clean/viewed/aliased/absent blocks, multiple 64KiB aliases, `writing`, `title_memory_written`, fault/open and remap; reference implementation validates exact dirty/notified pages and preserved writes after concurrent workers. Probe 1/2/4/8/16 mock writers and synthetic DStorage page notifications, including repeated 64KiB mappings. **Do NOT naively release `GUARDS` around `VirtualProtect`, page mapping, `inplace::pages_of` or `open`: the operation protects observable guest writes and requires generation/lock-order proof.** May benchmark bitmap/range caches against `BTreeSet` only with identical transitions. Actual hottest caller requires next Run16's new lock lines.
- [ ] **R64/D — DStorage scheduler offline benchmark and deterministic status/fairness fixtures (P0, no title).** Current `dstorage.rs::Lanes::start` scans active `HashMap` lanes to select class, then lane, and staging uses 32MiB; source ALREADY contains `priorities_share_by_bytes_about_tenfold`, `staging_bounds_compressed_bytes_in_flight`, `a_status_waits_for_every_earlier_read`, `a_queue_that_submits_itself_holds_reads_into_pages` and boundary-page tests. Extend rather than replace: fixed-seed 16 workers, 8–1024 lanes, 1k–100k varied-size jobs, priority and FIFO/status barriers, 32MiB staging pressure and intentionally delayed physical-page callbacks. Measure worker runnable/idle/queued, `Lanes` lock hold, scheduler selection µs/request, queue age p50/p95, throughput; compare current scan with a heap/index only if scan actually takes enough CPU. **Run15's 2.26s conditional queue wait does NOT prove the O(lanes) selector is the bottleneck; 200–450ms GPU-before/after-write notifications and GUARDS contention could dominate.** No early status completion, no altering page-list hold until guest Submit, no invented storage bandwidth gains.
- [ ] **R64/E — Low-noise asset provenance and benchmark automation (P1, no title).** Implement stable nonproprietary synthetic request/resource IDs and aggregate stage timestamps (queue, worker, read/decode, before/after guest page writes, GPU upload fence, descriptor generation, draw/indirect submit, first visible), plus a script that ingests existing Run12–15 CSV/log format and produces like-for-like **>=3k and 4500–5500 draws** p50/p90 frame/copyback/GPU-idle/VRAM bins with cache warmth and switch manifest. Verify parser on stored private derived data; keep raw game bytes and actual asset paths out of public commits, default diagnostic cost bounded. Nothing here makes assets load faster by itself; it establishes which stage to optimize in a subsequent batch.

**Source-backed other-emulator comparisons (new source audit; NO code was transplanted):**
| Emulator + inspected source | Actually demonstrated design | Defeat status / eligible improvement | Evidence/next gate |
|---|---|---|---|

Consider a pure synthetic *submission scheduling + fence lifetime timeline* model to identify safe earlier queue handoff only if Run16 demonstrates CPU-recorded-list starvation. | Verify exact fence/reference generation, no replay before guest order. Different console/GPU command processor, not direct transplant. |
| [**RPCS3** RSX texture cache](https://github.com/RPCS3/rpcs3/blob/master/rpcs3/Emu/RSX/Common/texture_cache.h) | Differentiates read versus write invalidation; protection checker and RO-cache exclusion assertions, unsynchronized ranges, per-frame flush/miss counters. | Defeat already tracks dirty journal, read-only physical views, `watch::writing`, `title_memory_written`, `VICTORY_LIGHT_REFRESH` (opt-in). **Potential**: add offline invariants for read-only preservation and alias metadata, not enable speculative skip of GPU read-back. | Synthetic dirty/protection state proof; no CPU/GPU write races or wrong hair/texture parity. |

Do not copy a Vulkan barrier model into D3D12 or weaken guest coherency. | Small independent simulator, validation, then PIX if D3D12 GPU timing actually critical. |

**Suggested bounded batch before next owner game test:** Claude works on (1) targeted write-back and guard safety fixtures, (2) compare/run small-write microbench, (3) deterministic scheduler queue/lock microbench, (4) a bounded collector for guard-call-site and asset-stage timing. Make any resulting performance edits opt-in and independently revertible; use `cargo test -p victory-gpu --lib -- --test-threads=1` and targeted `victory-hle` Windows tests, release builds and host synthetic GPU assertions. **Do not demand a game run after each source commit.** Then one Run16 on warm cache with fixed `VICTORY_WRITE_PLAIN=1`, `VICTORY_DIRTY_4K=0`, builder OFF, matched visual route; another fixed `=0` run only if needed to attribute write-back. Any dramatic unverified change stays disabled pending game validation. R62/R63 measured FPS/streaming priorities still govern; R64 adds a more efficient *development workflow*, not an unexplained new current FPS result.

### R65 — Gemini performance deep research audited against latest Defeat and cross-emulator sources (2026-11-28)

> **Input:** user pasted Gemini's ~43KB "Defeat Xbox Series X Emulator Performance Architecture and Critical-Path Investigation". Gemini explicitly said it could not open Defeat or extract owner R62 CSVs and therefore did NOT source-verify its "missing" features. **Source checked LIVE:** Defeat at main `b9b61c09` (last source edit `0c62860371d9a9212c74964f5725d489235e56e7`), `crates/victory-gpu/src/{host.rs,cardmeter.rs,queue2.rs,shadow.rs,watch.rs,plaincopy.rs,inplace.rs}`, `crates/victory-hle/src/modules/dstorage.rs`, `xtask/src/runstats.rs`, README, old R62–R64; upstream Xenia Canary readback option, RPCS3 async memory management issue and shadPS4 CPU/GPU memory tracker. This is **an evidence-backed research triage**, not an owner Run16 result and not permission to implement risky features without Claude's tests.

**Correct Gemini directions that survive source audit:**
- **Meaningful potential, unproven:** identify GPU-written resources that are **overwritten before any guest CPU observation**, then perhaps defer/cancel readbacks when exact alias, guest CPU read, storage IO, fence and write-back dependencies are known. This could remove a costly category of work; **measuring actually avoidable bytes/readbacks is first**, not immediately adding a global `GPU_OWNED` state. Exact guest CPU read interception and stale CPU/GPU memory mirror semantics may make broad fault-on-read impossible or counterproductive.
- **Meaningful potential, unproven:** diagram earliest command-list-ready and submission events, D3D12 direct/copy queue dependency graph, desc/guest-memory snapshots and fence lifetime to look for safe earlier kicks. **Parallel recording / async submission already partly implemented**; do not label it all missing or promise 4×–8× speedups.
- **Meaningful but gated:** optimize GUARDS lock and physical-page before/after notifications only after per-caller hold vs wait measured in a game; the latest deterministic scheduler benchmark (up to 1024 queues) showed selection under 200µs, while Run15 long waits are on a very different scale. **Do not mistake conditional queue wait for disk bandwidth or one-contended-mutex proof.**
- **Hardware-specific conditional:** if actual `VICTORY_WINDOW_UPLOAD` resource placement/fallback and VRAM headroom warrant it, study existing GPU_UPLOAD path with tiny benchmark. Windows/NVIDIA GPU_UPLOAD helps some CPU→GPU mapped writes, **not CPU reads of GPU-generated results**; Defeat already uses this heap type conditionally.

**Important factual fixes to Gemini's 12-item and 3-run roadmap:**
| Gemini recommendation or claim | Verified CURRENT Defeat source | Audit classification |
|---|---|---|
| "Implement GPU timestamp queries" / new `DEFEAT_GPU_TIMESTAMPS=1` | Already `cardmeter.rs` timestamp pairs, per-list kind/pipeline accounting, `VICTORY_CARD_METER=0/1` (defaults ON on diagnostics builds), and detailed `host.rs` `VICTORY_GPU_TIMING=1` (diagnostics build). **Detailed timing sends and waits for every list**; not normal FPS. | **ALREADY IMPLEMENTED — use/extend measured gaps only**. `DEFEAT_*` invented, real prefix `VICTORY_*`. |
| "Adopt `D3D12_HEAP_TYPE_GPU_UPLOAD`" | `host.rs::gpu_upload_buffer` makes and CPU-maps GPU_UPLOAD committed resource after `D3D12_OPTIONS16`; validates mapped reads/writes; `shadow.rs::window_upload_for` uses it, `VICTORY_WINDOW_UPLOAD` source defaults ON (if supported and budget admits). | **ALREADY IMPLEMENTED — optimize eligibility/fallback only**. Existing VRAM pressure means no blanket placement. |

| "DirectStorage I/O+decoder holds global guard-lock, redesign 3 stages" | `dstorage.rs::read_into` times file reading separately; `into_destination` times `kernel_will_write` / `pages_will_write` before physical-page fill and `pages_written` afterward. `VICTORY_PAGE_WRITES_BEFORE` exists; scheduler weighted priorities, staging, status FIFO and held-until-Submit behavior already implemented and tested. | **SPECULATIVE ROOT CAUSE**: measured long before/after notifications, not proof lock spans all disk I/O. First correlate `watch::guards_line` + xmem page-map callbacks, then test safe narrow changes. |

Defeat alias/guard/reprotect behavior differs. | **HIGH RISK RESEARCH**, no default deferred page protection without exact state and every observer proof. |

Keep as staged architecture research, separate from stable baseline. |
| "Set four new DEFEAT_* switches for Run17/18 in one batch" | None of Gemini's proposed names has been source-confirmed; existing tools use `VICTORY_*`. Four coherency/queue feature rewrites in one game experiment defeat correct attribution and may corrupt memory. | **REJECT bulk plan**. Make single opt-in feature with rollback and golden source/fence byte parity before each game trial. |

**Mathematical correction:** Gemini writes `203.4ms median frame - 99.7ms median GPU no-work = 103.7ms "GPU-queued window"` and then interprets it as potential shader-execution budget. **Difference of medians need not equal median of differences**, and even a true queued interval is **not necessarily full active shader execution**. CPU/GPU timelines, queue fences and GPU timestamp spans must be aligned per corresponding frame, covering direct and copy queues, idle gaps and wait dependencies. The stated 75–95ms GPU floor, 25–45ms pure shader time, and 10–20FPS outcomes are **illustrative hypothetical regimes**, not RTX4070 measurements. Substantial reductions in wall-frame time need evidence of *non-overlapping critical-path* savings, not totals of partially concurrent threads.

**Cross-emulator evidence and important caveats:**
- [Xenia Canary GPU option documentation](https://github.com/xenia-canary/xenia-canary/wiki/Options) confirms `d3d12_readback_resolve` defaults OFF but explicitly notes some **Xbox 360** games need it. This is NOT proof that title-memory-dependent Series X guest readback can be disabled. Safer translation: trace real consumers, validate an exact overwrite-discard for a provably dead region.
- [shadPS4 memory tracker](https://github.com/shadps4-emu/shadPS4/blob/main/src/video_core/buffer_cache/memory_tracker.h) differentiates CPU/GPU-modified regions and inspires a reference state machine; Defeat already has similar write journal/protected alias concepts. Need complete page-lifetime and per-view reference proof to adapt.
- [RPCS3 #16800](https://github.com/RPCS3/rpcs3/issues/16800) discusses `g_deferred_mprotect_queue` and explicitly calls out visual corruption and forced barriers, including updated 2026 observations. Not a turnkey Defeat speedup.
- [Microsoft D3D12 GPU upload heap spec](https://microsoft.github.io/DirectX-Specs/d3d/D3D12GPUUploadHeaps.html) and [NVIDIA](https://developer.nvidia.com/blog/optimizing-dx12-resource-uploads-to-the-gpu-using-gpu-upload-heaps/) support CPU-writable VRAM as a valid upload strategy, but it is already code in Defeat and does not make GPU→CPU reads free. No verified win from extra GPU_UPLOAD residency on this particular scene.

Each independent candidate requires Windows host verification and can be separately backed out. A separate fixed writeback-off comparison may be useful. **Do not use detailed `VICTORY_GPU_TIMING` FPS as normal release FPS.**

**Priority disposition:** Gemini **does not demonstrate** an immediate 2× or 4× optimization. **Top near-term: measure and reduce the actual submitter/guards/DirectStorage critical path in the new R64 code**. Top longer-term architectural research: prove which GPU readbacks are avoidable and whether earlier submission preserves exact guest synchronization. | Picture and ray state | Hair-block artifact not observed in Run15 with DIRTY4K OFF; causation not proved. Four shadow PSOs built but **none first-bound**, fifth `dbdbf36298727135` fails at 0xae80; **26** distinct Wave64 PSO refusals; builder OFF. | **P2 correctness track**, not a claimed near-term FPS win or reason to enable DIRTY4K/BVH by default. |

**Latest implementation reconciliation (SRC, new since R65):** `4a8acbe` already fixes release-mode `cardmeter::collect()` reachability when frame logging is disabled; adds `VICTORY_READBACK_REUSE=1` (OFF by default) in `shadow.rs`, with sharded per-64-KiB-page last-write stamps and **own-view write-back bytes** classified as again/touched/first; extends `runstats` to report card-running duration, count-weighted slow-storage stages and the reuse classification. It **does not defer any readback**, discover guest CPU reads, or prove a skipped copy would preserve memory coherence. The observer examines a subset of write-backs (own-view path), and **unseen guest CPU reads mean `again` is only an upper-bound candidate, not redundant or safely removable bytes**. Page-granular grouping and in-flight aliases/remaps further require validation. R64 seed tests, guard instrumentation, fixed slowest-request stage logging, DirectStorage scheduler test and runstats already exist: do **not** duplicate the implementation effort.

**Current actionable gates (leave unchecked until concrete evidence exists):**

- [ ] **R66/P0-A — OWNER + CLAUDE: Run16 fixed release baseline on latest build.** Preserve warm cache, same reproducible story room/route/visual comparison, `VICTORY_BVH_BUILDER=0`, `VICTORY_DIRTY_4K=0`, `VICTORY_WRITE_PLAIN=1`, no `alt`, no high-overhead detailed GPU timing. Record **source SHA**, precise switches, driver/background GPU load, run duration, session point, same-camera hair/skin/picture, device loss/DRED and `cargo xtask runstats` summaries for >=3,000 and 4,500–5,500 draws (N, p50/p90 frame, preparing, copy-back, queue-empty, submission busy, budget). Compare against Run15 descriptively, **not causally** unless scene/settings are matched. **Done when:** the real post-Run15 build has a valid owner-hardware baseline and correctness record.
- [ ] **R66/P0-B — CLAUDE: independently attribute the remaining storage/guard/submitter path without another blind optimization.** On a short *separate diagnostic capture* use the **already available** `watch::guards_line`, repaired DStorage `slowest_parts`, existing queue timings, and `VICTORY_CARD_METER=1` to distinguish **request queued → worker granted → BEFORE page notification → guest-page write/decode → AFTER notification → graphics resource ready → list submitted → GPU fence**. Report high-percentile **individual request** values rather than sum of rounded conditional means; identify exact guard callsite wait vs hold and physical page-map wait, not just the global worker count. **Done when:** one top cause is pinned to a specific owned lock/stage and can be tested or rejected independently; keep guest before/after order and status FIFO.
- [ ] **R66/P0-C — CLAUDE + RESEARCH: validate timeline/profiler interpretation before claiming GPU savings.** `cardmeter.rs` intentionally reads timestamps after a delay; `runstats.rs` nearest-time joins aggregate lines, and card-running time is not automatically same-frame GPU execution or the union of concurrent direct/copy queues. Compare parser output with saved Run15 CSV/JSON and with controlled synthetic log fixtures; require N/phase labels and matching time scopes. For a real cross-queue critical-path model, verify queue-specific frequency, supported query-heap type, CPU/GPU clock calibration and completed-fence attribution. Microsoft [D3D12 Timing](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/direct3d12/timing) specifies **per-queue** timestamp frequency/calibration and distinct copy-queue timestamp support; [D3D12 readback](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/direct3d12/readback-data-using-heaps) requires explicit GPU completion/fence handling. **Do not subtract medians**, sum overlapping thread work, or run `VICTORY_GPU_TIMING=1` as an ordinary FPS benchmark. **Done when:** parsed values match reference inputs and each timeline claim has aligned per-event evidence.
- [ ] **R66/P1-D — CLAUDE: characterize the observer's useful bound, safely and separately.** Run `VICTORY_READBACK_REUSE=1` as its own short observational capture (off for baseline), quantify overhead, per-window **again/touched/first** counts, own-view coverage vs kernel fallback, page identity/remap generation, and first CPU/IO/GPU consumer timing. Require synthetic alias/partial writes, page-map change and direct/copy fence cases before discussing any deferred/overwrite-cancel readback. **Done when:** an independently reproducible upper-bound histogram, observer cost, and explicitly unknowable guest-read fraction are recorded; **no lazy readback enabled**.
- [ ] **R66/P1-E — CLAUDE + GEMINI: investigate one delayed visible building/object end-to-end.** Ask for nonproprietary stable request/resource IDs and timestamps from I/O worker grant through guest-map, descriptor/texture or buffer residency, PSO, indirect draw, and first visible frame, with fence/snapshot safety. Compare 8,642 queued texture slots and sparse tile-pool headroom to exact delayed resource rather than inferring disk latency or prefetch failure. No game bytes/paths in public diagnostics. **Done when:** one specific late scene asset is classified as storage, guest map, CPU submit, card capacity, pipeline or rendering dependence.
- [ ] **R66/P2-F — GEMINI research and CLAUDE decision gate.** Deep-research **only** (a) real D3D12 direct/copy queue timestamp and sync semantics applicable to current cardmeter/QUEUE2; (b) readback consumer-observability limits from Xenia/RSX/shadPS4/RPCS3 compared with Defeat's title-memory aliasing; (c) independent VRAM tile-pool/heap residency methods on a ~12-GB card. For every proposal give a primary-source URL, inspected implementation or API claim, what Defeat *already* implements, potential correctness counterexample, bounded synthetic experiment and measurable stop/go threshold. **Done when:** source-backed questions/answers and a safe single-variable plan are appended; no generic "2×–8×" or predicted FPS.

**Hand-off order:** **Claude (coder)**: finish the reproducible game baseline and targeted critical-path/observer analysis using already committed instrumentation, test one bounded implementation hypothesis at a time if evidence supports it, keep any risky changes opt-in and revertible. **Gemini (deep researcher)**: investigate the R66/P2-F primary-source questions and enumerate counterexamples/uncertainties; do not prescribe code or new switches that already exist. **Research assistant**: reconcile their evidence in this checklist, prioritize the next A/B and mark only gates with actual results. One controlled title run after an offline batch is enough; do **not** require a full owner game run after every isolated synthetic test.

### R67 — Run16 owner log and GPU audit versus Run15: next Claude/Gemini research gates (2026-11-28; research-only)

> **Evidence/authority:** Owner attachments `run16.zip` (one `run16.txt` log), `gpu16.csv` (UTF-16 NVIDIA telemetry), and a pasted, longer overlapping NVIDIA telemetry export. Independently parsed **33,210 emulator log lines, 105 five-second frame summary windows, 510 approx-one-second clock/guard/copy-back windows, 108 DirectStorage report windows and 47 detailed slow-request breakdown windows**. Reference Run15 numbers are the previously owner-supplied `Defeat_R62_Run15_Analysis_Summary.json` and joined CSVs, not game-run data recreated by this assistant. **No attached screenshots or matched camera/route proof.** Do not publish raw game log/path or title bytes. These are window-aggregate observations, not individual-frame percentiles or laboratory A/B measurements.

**Build and diagnostic manifest (Run16):** `run16.txt` begins **2026-11-28 01:43:34 UTC**, ends **01:53:36 UTC**; build prints `Series X|S 1.0.2.1`, **`source 3665d56781bc`**; the sole displayed explicit switch is **`VICTORY_BVH_BUILDER=0`**. That source is the research-only commit immediately after Claude's [`4a8acbe`](https://github.com/Zaxix/Defeat/commit/4a8acbe7189997403287e4d4925348692adb5118); it includes R64/R65's new write-back implementation and profiler. **No separate `VICTORY_WRITE_PLAIN=alt` phase or `VICTORY_DIRTY_4K=1` is reported**. The run logs normal `VICTORY_WRITE_PLAIN` write activity. No `VICTORY_CARD_METER` per-kind or `VICTORY_READBACK_REUSE` line exists: **do not call the absent observer/timestamp results zero**. The log terminates after pipeline library save; there is no device-lost or watchdog-crash message in the provided capture, but run termination and full visual correctness cannot be inferred.

**Direct Run15 → Run16 performance comparison (frame report windows with >=3,000 draws, Python direct extraction from log; p90 by interpolated quantile):**

| Statistic | Run15 older `be0ed3b7` (alternating WRITE_PLAIN ON/OFF) | Run16 `3665d567` (normal no-alt switches reported) | Interpretation |
|---|---:|---:|---|
| Valid report windows | 67 | 60 | Similar count, **not the same frame or scene** |
| Median / p90 frame time | **203.4 / 278.4 ms** | **168.0 / 336.5 ms** | Median **17.4% lower**, p90 **20.9% higher**. Reciprocal of median ~4.92 → ~5.95 FPS, not measured average instantaneous FPS |
| Median draws/window | 5,588 | 6,034.5 | Composition shifted; grouping by draw count alone cannot control for scene, view or background workload |
| Median preparing | 109.6 ms | 96.7 ms | Smaller subphase in this sample |
| Median copy-back | 76.0 ms | **43.3 ms** | **43.0% smaller**, consistent with post-Run15 changes but not causal attribution by this test |
| Median copy preparation / texture refresh | 42.0 / 5.9 ms | 45.45 / 7.3 ms | Not universally faster; copying work and frame composition differ |
| Nearest-clock median: GPU queue empty / empty while submitter busy | 99.7 / 77.8 ms | **85.35 / 74.1 ms** | Important CPU-side starvation persists; values are periodic clock aggregates joined to adjacent frame-report windows, not per-frame exact clock traces |
| Nearest guard-line median: all-thread waits / holds | not instrumented comparably in supplied Run15 pack | **51.1 / 25.8 ms** | Aggregated across threads; never subtract them mechanically from wall-frame time |
| Nearest merge-line median: write-back / own-view / kernel guard-open / old write | 117.7 / 47.8 / 8.0 / 48.5 ms | **47.4 / 13.95 / 9.4 / 19.05 ms** | Promising total and own-view CPU work reduction; merges are time summed over workers, **not isolated wall-critical path** |

**Tail-latency check (why the median alone is insufficient):** In a narrower **4,500–5,500 draws/window** band, Run15 **N=14, frame p50/p90 178.05/232.62 ms** and Run16 **N=9, 193.3/372.56 ms**; these small unmatched samples **do not confirm** a game FPS uplift. Run16's all-window p90 **541.3ms** includes long loading/low-draw intervals; one **2,032.9ms** frame-report window at 1,192 draws and repeated >=700ms windows require separate phase/workload classification rather than treating them as ordinary steady-world frames. In >=3k windows, Run16 p90 **336.5ms** and maximum **808.4ms** demand frame-correlated evidence to distinguish preparation, GPU, storage, game logic and queue stalls.

**DirectStorage and guard locks (Run16):** Across 108 storage reporting windows, **1,475 plain + 188 BCPack = 1,663 requests >=250ms**, **16 plain + 1 BCPack = 17 >1s**; the separately instrumented 47 breakdown windows report these same **1,663** slow requests. Slow-request **count-weighted conditional means**: wait for worker **~1,119ms**, file read **~0.4ms**, before-write notification **~241ms**, decode/copy **~0.3ms**, after-write/physical-page work **~165ms**. Run15 conditional means were **~2,263 / ~0.04 / ~271 / ~0.72 / ~193ms**, with **1,701 slow >=250ms and 71 >1s**. Different load/scenes, so **do not claim causal 2× queue improvement or faster disk**. The Run16 guard tracker across all 510 short reporting windows has median **43.95ms waited / 24.05ms held across all threads**, with waits sometimes exceeding 200ms aggregated. Frequent wait contributors are `watch.rs` lock call sites around **line 1870** (`writing`, kernel/guest write notification) and **line 2162** (vectored write-fault handler); much held time is near **line 1544** (guard range opening). The exact named source line may move after subsequent commits; source-audit with SHA before changing locks. None of these sums establishes that the guard lock was on the sole critical thread.

**GPU telemetry and memory (Run16):** `gpu16.csv` contains **690 ~1-second NVIDIA samples from 01:43:23–01:55:01 UTC**, covering the emulator log; the pasted longer sample is **overlapping supplemental telemetry** (its first text row is malformed), not a second performance run. During the approx. **01:45:30–01:53:36** high-workload period, **480 samples** show **GPU utilization p50 32%, p90 97%**, sampled device VRAM usage p50 **11,266.5 MiB**, max **11,588 MiB**, and mostly PCIe Gen4 x16 active. Utilization is **device-wide**, not a per-emulator GPU execution trace: peak 97% and low median mean bursts coexist with CPU/queue stalls. Internal Run16 host memory reports at 105 windows: median local usage **10,305 MB**, budget **10,993 MB**, and **minimum cross-window budget-minus-peak-use proxy 352 MB**; that proxy is not an exact same-instant free-byte measurement. **GPU-local residency remains a constraint**, but this run does not justify blanket automatic VRAM migration or unrelated GPU_UPLOAD increases.

**Rendering/safety observations:** Run16 reports **26 distinct 64-wave compute PSO refusals**, consistent with historical Wave64 problem; the four shadow shaders `002e93a6cc2aa355`, `07c93bfb77fecebb`, `0e64f14fae060d04`, `96ccb14ef981fd85` translated and received host pipelines but **no first-bind event matching their hashes was seen**. Fifth `dbdbf36298727135` still fails at GFX10 offset `0xae80` with lane-mask-as-data. Among >=3k windows, **builder-off drops median 443/frame**, refused/untranslated median **3/frame**, pipeline wait median **0**. Thus dropped draws primarily reflect intentionally disabled ray-tracing builder; DO NOT re-enable it for this benchmark merely to suppress drops. A separate texture picked by index **changed after upload without notification** warning recurs; its cumulative counter reached **92,001** near end, **not necessarily 92,001 unique textures**. Test visual timing and invalidation coherency before speculative `DIRTY_4K`, lazy readback, or broader memory policy changes.

### R68 — Cross-emulator native-FPS research: two user-linked forks and prioritized porting gates (2026-11-28; research-only)

> **User directive/ownership:** Investigate the actual source of [trustno1-coder/VIctory-NVIDIA](https://github.com/trustno1-coder/VIctory-NVIDIA) and [DexNom/VIctory](https://github.com/DexNom/VIctory), plus other relevant emulators, to improve **real game FPS** on owner's RTX 4070 / Ryzen 7 5800X / 64 GiB. Research assistant changes **this checklist only**; **Claude owns all code, integration and tests**, Gemini independent primary-source deep research. No emulator source file or implementation-status table is changed here. This is a scope-targeted audit of major applicable codebases, **not a claim that literally every emulator ever written was inspected**. Status tags: **SRC** = inspected live source; **AUTHOR** = fork author's documented measurements, not independently repeated; **RUN16** = owner's previous log audit R67; **HYP** = proposed, unmeasured in Defeat.

**Snapshot and fair benchmark rule.** Sources inspected at their accessible default-branch trees: NVIDIA `a4d05e7` (`main`), DexNom `4ad8692` (`main`), fxceless `2f3f399` (`main`), upstream Marvisak `63bcca4` (`main`) and Defeat as of research R67, `0624770` (`main`, research-only). Recency, shader cache/warm-up, launch script, actual build and switch defaults require re-check before any experiment. Source tree layouts alone do **not** prove a feature absent: compare behavior in the corresponding function, not only filename/switch. Run16 (Defeat `3665d567`, builder OFF, normal release): >=3k draws windows **N=60 p50 168.0ms / p90 336.5ms**, preparation **96.7ms**, copy-back **43.3ms**, GPU queue empty **85.35ms** (~74.1ms while submitter active). These are aggregated windows, not matched per-present critical-path samples; R67 remains the evidence for owner Run16.

BVH builder OFF; ambient/reflection estimate replaces rays, some effects deliberately omitted; streaming hitches acknowledged. | Highly motivating **feasibility lead**, not an A/B: faster CPU/GPU, scenes, resolution, light approximation, optional omissions and build differ. Authors warn routes/scenes differ. Also `VICTORY_DYNAMIC_WAIT` describes avoiding ~13ms/frame lock hold (up to ~48ms with ray tracing) while waiting for dynamic descriptor slots. | Closest GPU class to owner and direct motivation to study architecture. **Not a controlled FPS gain** or proof Defeat has the same lock: Defeat's `HostHeap::dynamic` currently returns `None` and reports refused tables, not necessarily an identical wait loop. | [NVIDIA foxhound findings](https://github.com/trustno1-coder/VIctory-NVIDIA/blob/main/docs/findings.md) (AUTHOR) | Earlier 7900 XTX / i9-12900K diagnostics runs reduced typical frame from 280ms toward 46–65ms in different stages. Logged changes include descriptor work shifted later, parallel preparation, `VICTORY_THIN`, safer copied dwords, guarded write avoidance. They explicitly say stages overlap and different scenes vary. | Treat as design proof-of-concept, not additive milliseconds or RTX4070 benchmark.
